## Graveyard

*Ideas killed on investigation, with the reason — the tuition file.*

- **Vivendi mandatory-tender catalyst** — died 2026-07-08. Paris Court of Appeal ruled *no Bolloré control* (control read narrowly as votes actually exercised); stock -10-12%; AMF tender off the table; CIAM's fresh cassation appeal is a 12+ month lottery. **Tuition:** French appellate binaries are real coin flips even with the advocate general on your side — the event risk materialized; a losing outcome alone does not validate the original probability or sizing assumptions.
- **Two Harbors bump thesis** — resolved 2026-07-02. Shareholders approved CCM $12.00 despite ISS/GL opposition; UWM's $12.50 never got board traction. **Tuition:** proxy-adviser opposition does not defeat a vote when the register is retail/income-heavy and the board controls the adjournment clock (four adjournments until the votes were there).
- **GDV Saba board seat** — died 2026-06-29. All three Gabelli nominees elected; Saba's nominee lost despite ISS backing. **Tuition:** family-controlled CEF complexes with loyal retail bases defeat activist arithmetic; without a forcing mechanism the discount is not a catalyst, it's a fact.
- **WEX insider-cluster pillar** — killed 2026-07-02 (entry retained as WATCH on valuation). Form 4 "purchases" were bonus-paid-in-stock grants, not open-market buys. **Tuition:** always distinguish transaction codes on Form 4s (P vs A/F) before calling something an insider cluster.
- **Burford "free YPF option"** — killed 2026-07-02 (entry retained as WATCH). Stock traded at a *premium* to ex-YPF book, not a discount. **Tuition:** "the market values X at zero" claims must be arithmetic, not vibes — compute the stub before believing it.

<a id="1-spinoffs-corporate-separations"></a>

## 1. Spinoffs & Corporate Separations

**Why the edge exists:** Spinoff alpha persists because the selling is mechanical, not fundamental: index funds must dump spincos that don't qualify for the parent's index (or are too small), and the parent's shareholder base — which self-selected for the parent's profile — receives shares in a business it never chose to own (S&P Global's quality-compounder holders receiving an auto-data company; Aptiv's EV-tech growth holders receiving a wire-harness maker). Spincos also arrive with no roadshow, thin sell-side coverage, and messy pro-forma financials, so price discovery takes one to four quarters while management incentives (options struck at post-spin lows) are set to maximize later performance. The mid-2026 window is unusually rich: HONA, FDXF, VGNT, VSNT, and MBGL all listed within the last six months, and a second wave (Vylor/Corteva, ADI/Resideo, MiniMed split-off, KDP coffee split) is scheduled for 2H 2026.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Weekly EDGAR full-text search for new and amended Form 10-12B registration statements (the spinco 'Form 10') — read the information statement for distribution ratio, spin debt, cash dividends to parent, and management incentive strike dates
- Track 8-Ks announcing board approval of separations plus record/distribution dates; monitor when-issued (WI) trading versus regular-way for early price-discovery gaps (e.g., Versant WI at $55 vs $40.57 regular-way close)
- Maintain a calendar from stockspinoffs.com, InsideArbitrage's spinoff page, and stockanalysis.com/actions/spinoffs — cross-check each name against S&P/Nasdaq index committee announcements to time forced index selling and deletion dates
- Screen completed spins 1–12 months post-distribution trading below both parent and closest pure-play peer on EV/EBITDA and P/FCF, combined with open-market insider buying (Form 4s filed within 90 days of the spin)
- Watch parent retained stakes for supply overhang and structured exits: FedEx kept 19.9% of FDXF (must dispose, likely within ~a year); Medtronic kept ~90% of MiniMed pending a split-off — SC TO-I exchange-offer filings historically offer a ~7% discount plus odd-lot priority (no proration under 100 shares)
- For announced-but-pending separations, build sum-of-parts on the parent before the Form 10 pro formas make it consensus (Corteva/Vylor, Resideo/ADI, Keurig Dr Pepper Beverage Co vs Global Coffee Co)
- Include foreign/dual-listed spins where US index and geographic mandates amplify selling (Magnum Ice Cream from Unilever; Continental's Aumovio) and antitrust-forced divestitures from DOJ/FTC/EC remedies

**Candidates:**

### [A] Mobility Global (S&P Global spinoff — CARFAX, Polk, automotiveMastermind) — `MBGL (NYSE)`
- **Thesis:** Spun from S&P Global on July 1, 2026 (yesterday) via 1:1 distribution — an 80%+ subscription-revenue data business (CARFAX) being dumped by SPGI's index and quality-compounder shareholder base that owns SPGI for ratings/indices, not auto data.
- **Key numbers:** 2025 revenue ~$1.75B; 2025 adj EBITDA $711M (LTM ~$724M); FCF $461M; net debt ~$1.85B (2.6x); handicapped fair range low-$20s (12x EBITDA) to low-$30s (16x) per stockspinoffs.com analysis — actual first-week trading price unverified
- **Catalyst:** Forced index/mandate selling exhausts in first weeks of July; first standalone earnings report; sell-side initiation coverage; likely index reshuffle (probable S&P 500 deletion) creating a defined selling event
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Auto retail cyclicality and dealer consolidation pressure CARFAX/Mastermind; 2.6x leverage on a newly standalone cost structure; if it opens already at 14-16x EBITDA the mispricing never materializes
- **Next steps:** Pull first regular-way prices and volume now; read the MBGL information statement (Form 10-12B) for segment margins and standalone cost dis-synergies; confirm index treatment (S&P announcement); model at 12x/14x/16x EBITDA vs Verisk/CoStar/CCC comps; check Form 4s for management buys in first 90 days
- **Status:** **PURSUE** — dossier 2026-07-02 ([ideas/mobility-global.md](ideas/mobility-global.md)). 07-13 update: $20.80 (Jul 10) after a $22.26 bounce — deletion flowback mostly absorbed, tape still sloppy; entry window open; ~25% below street average target ($27.67).

### [A] Versant Media Group (Comcast cable networks spinoff) — `VSNT (Nasdaq)`
- **Thesis:** Classic Greenblatt 'bad-co' spin: CNBC, MS NOW, USA, Golf Channel dumped 14% on day one (Jan 5, 2026) and prices at roughly 5-6x guided free cash flow — a melting ice cube priced for near-immediate death while management returns cash.
- **Key numbers:** Debut close $40.57 = $5.9B market cap (mid-May ~$40); FY2026 guide: adj EBITDA $1.85-2.0B, FCF $1.0-1.2B (company 8-K); $100M buybacks completed + $100M more authorized; $1.50/sh annualized dividend; net debt at spin unverified
- **Catalyst:** Capital returns (buyback + ~3.7% dividend at debut price); potential consolidation of cable network assets after Paramount-Skydance absorbs WBD (DOJ approved June 2026); first full-year FCF proof point
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Cord-cutting decelerates FCF faster than guided; news/sports rights cost inflation; CNBC digital transition fails; this is a declining asset — thesis dies if FCF guide is cut materially in the next two quarters
- **Next steps:** Verify balance sheet debt from the Q1 2026 8-K/10-Q (CIK 2067876); reconcile the $704M Q1 adj EBITDA print against the $1.85-2.0B FY guide (seasonality of sports rights); model a runoff DCF at -5%/-10% annual FCF decline vs current EV; compare to WBD Global Networks takeout multiples
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [B] FedEx remainco (post-Freight stub) — `FDX (NYSE)`
- **Thesis:** FDXF listed June 1, 2026 at a rich ~24x adjusted operating income (~$22.6B equity at ~$152), implying the market pays LTL-scarcity prices for the spinco — making the FDX Express/Ground stub, which also received $4.1B cash and retains 19.9% of FDXF, the cheap side of the separation.
- **Key numbers:** FDXF: ~$152/sh, ~$22.6B equity, ~$26.7B EV, ~$8.7B FY26 revenue, ~$1.1B adj operating income (~3.1x sales, ~24x adj op income); FDX received $4.1B cash + retained 19.9% of FDXF; implied stub EV and stub multiple unverified — must be computed
- **Catalyst:** First post-spin FDX quarterly report re-baselines the stub P&L; monetization of the retained 19.9% FDXF stake (likely debt-for-equity exchange or secondary within ~a year); DRIVE cost program flowing through a cleaner income statement
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Express is structurally challenged (B2B parcel decline, trade/tariff volatility); the stub may deserve a low multiple; FDXF retained-stake disposal is an overhang on FDXF, not FDX; a freight recession hits both sides
- **Next steps:** Compute stub math: FDX market cap minus value of 19.9% FDXF stake and $4.1B cash vs pro-forma remainco EBIT from the June 2026 Form 8-K pro formas; compare stub multiple to UPS; read FDXF Form 10 for inter-company service agreements that could leak value
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [B] Versigent (Aptiv electrical distribution spinoff) — `VGNT (NYSE)`
- **Thesis:** Aptiv's growth/EV-tech shareholder base dumped the 'boring' wire-harness spinco from ~$45 to $26.34 within days of the April 1, 2026 spin — a top-3 player in a Yazaki/Sumitomo oligopoly (60-70% combined share of a ~$70B market) that has recovered to ~$46 but may still trade at a mid-single-digit EBITDA multiple.
- **Key numbers:** ~$45.89/sh, ~$3.3B market cap; $2.1B spin debt ($2.125B cash distribution to Aptiv); 2026 revenue guide $9.1-9.4B; post-spin low $26.34 (Apr 9, 2026); EBITDA and current EV/EBITDA multiple unverified (UBS/press cite ~5x)
- **Catalyst:** First two standalone earnings prints; sell-side initiations (UBS buy already); content-per-vehicle growth as EV harness content roughly doubles vs ICE; possible index inclusion at ~$3B+ cap
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Deeply cyclical auto production; low-single-digit operating margins leave no room for error; Mexico labor inflation and tariff/USMCA disruption; customer concentration with big OEMs; the easy 75% off the low is already gone
- **Next steps:** Read Versigent Form 10-12B/A and Q1 2026 8-K (CIK 2078008) to pin down actual EBITDA, margin, and net debt; compare to Leoni/Yazaki private comps and Aptiv's old segment disclosure; check insider Form 4 buys post-spin; model downside at trough auto SAAR
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [B] Corteva pre-spin sum-of-parts / Vylor seed spinco — `CTVA (NYSE); Vylor ticker TBD`
- **Thesis:** Corteva separates its crown-jewel Pioneer seed business (renamed Vylor, Form 10 already filed) from crop protection in 2H 2026 — buy the parent before the Form 10 pro formas make the sum-of-parts consensus, and prepare for the crop-protection remainco to be orphaned as the 'liability co.'

- **Next steps:** Read Vylor Form 10-12B and DRS filings (SEC CIK 2128626) for segment pro formas and liability allocation; build SOTP on CTVA using Bayer Crop Science/FMC comps for remainco and a seed-royalty multiple for Vylor; track WI trading when dates are set

- **Thesis:** Resideo spins its low-margin security-products distribution arm (ADI) in 2H 2026, leaving a cleaner Honeywell Home products remainco — a small-cap two-for-one where neither piece fits the current holder base and the pre-spin SOTP is obscured by the distribution/products mash-up.

<a id="2-insider-buying-clusters-share-cannibals"></a>

## 2. Insider Buying Clusters & Share Cannibals

**Why the edge exists:** Cluster insider buying is legal transmission of private information: the academic record (Lakonishok & Lee 2001; Cohen/Malloy/Pomorski) shows opportunistic multi-insider purchases predict ~7.5% 12-month outperformance, concentrated in small/mid caps. The edge persists for three structural reasons. First, clusters appear precisely when the public narrative is ugliest (proxy fights, DOJ probes, earnings misses), so career-risk-constrained institutions won't touch the names even when the people with the best information are writing personal checks. Second, most clusters occur below the liquidity floor of large funds, and sell-side coverage thins exactly as the signal fires. Third, cannibals shrinking their float 8-15%/year are systematically under-modeled: consensus screens sort on absolute net-income growth, not per-share FCF growth, so the mechanical compounding of a cheap stock retiring 10% of itself annually never shows up in a growth screen — it only shows up in the share count, which almost nobody screens on.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Weekly Form 4 cluster screen: open-market purchases (transaction code P), >=3 distinct insiders within 14 days, >=$100K each, excluding 10b5-1 plans and comp-linked acquisitions (read footnotes). Sources: OpenInsider cluster-buy screen, insider-monitor.com, QuiverQuant insider feed, GuruFocus insider cluster page, 13radar.com/insider/cluster-buys, MarketTriage Form 4 tracker.
- Cannibal screen: share count down >8% y/y (or >20% over 3 years), FCF yield >8%, net debt/EBITDA <2.5x, and net repurchases (buybacks minus SBC issuance) positive. Sources: stockanalysis.com statistics pages (shares-outstanding change), westmountfundamentals.com/buyback-leaders (106 names ranked), stocktitan.net top-share-buyback list, financecharts.com repurchase history.
- Intersection list first: names on BOTH screens (insiders buying while the company shrinks its float) are the highest-conviction pond — WEX and FCN both surfaced this way in May-June 2026.
- Watch 8-Ks announcing buyback authorizations >10% of market cap and SC TO-I Dutch tender filings; an authorization sized like WEX's $1B on a ~$5.5B cap is a screaming board signal.
- Fallen large caps: stocks 40%+ off highs where DIRECTORS (not just the CEO) buy in size — director clusters are rarer and statistically stronger; the UNH 10-director cluster of April 1, 2026 is the template.
- Timing: re-run screens in the 2-6 weeks after each earnings season (mid-Feb, mid-May, mid-Aug, mid-Nov) when insider trading windows open — clusters concentrate there.
- Hygiene: always pull the actual Form 4s from EDGAR full-text search to confirm price, size relative to the insider's existing stake and salary, and that the buy was truly open-market.

<a id="3-quality-compounders-at-temporary-discounts"></a>

## 3. Quality Compounders at Temporary Discounts

**Why the edge exists:** This edge persists because institutional investors face career risk owning a franchise while headlines are bad: momentum and growth funds are forced sellers the moment a compounder's growth stalls, and value funds hesitate because the stock still screens "expensive" on trailing optics. The market systematically extrapolates one to two bad years into a permanent impairment (recency bias), even when ROIC, unit economics, and market share remain intact. Time-horizon arbitrage does the rest: most of the market prices the next 2-4 quarters, so an investor willing to underwrite a 3-5 year normalization captures both the earnings recovery and the multiple re-rating. The pattern is verifiable in mid-2026: UnitedHealth (+45% off lows, Berkshire already exited) and Texas Instruments (+29% YTD) show how fast the discount closes once the "temporary" nature becomes consensus.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Quant screen: 5-yr average ROIC >20%, gross margin stable or rising over 10 yrs, FCF conversion >80%, net debt/EBITDA <2x, AND price >30% below 52-week high while consensus FY+2 EPS estimates fell less than half as much as the price — the gap is the extrapolation error.
- Morningstar wide-moat coverage: pull the quarterly list of wide-moat names trading at the largest discount to fair value (Nike was flagged at 53% below FVE in June 2026); use it as a starting universe, not a conclusion.
- Capitulation markers in filings: 8-Ks announcing dividend cuts (Diageo Feb 2026), CEO replacements (Nestle, Diageo, Novo), or activist/proxy events (Lululemon 2026 proxy battle) at businesses with >20% ROIC — these mark the point of maximum institutional selling.
- 13F cloning: track new or added positions in drawdown names from Berkshire, Fundsmith, Akre, Giverny, Polen, TCI, Pershing Square each quarter; a quality-focused holder initiating into a 40%+ decline is a strong second-opinion signal.
- Form 4 insider-buying clusters within 90 days of a >35% drawdown, especially at founder/family-controlled firms (Copart, Brown-Forman, Nestle executive director purchase in 2026).
- Sell-side inflection watch: upgrades explicitly citing 'valuation' after a >40% decline (JPMorgan upgraded Chipotle in June 2026 on exactly this logic) tend to mark the start of narrative repair.
- Event/regulatory calendars to date the catalyst: FHFA/GSE credit-score adoption milestones (FICO), FDA approval and launch dates (Novo oral Wegovy Jan 2026), DOJ/litigation dockets, quarterly total-loss frequency data from CCC/ISS for Copart.
- 52-week-low list filtered for S&P 500 / STOXX 600 members with 10-yr revenue CAGR >5% and >15 consecutive years of positive FCF — quality rarely appears on the new-low list; when it does, investigate within the week.

- **Thesis:** A 100-year duopolist in diabetes/obesity trading at roughly half its historical multiple because the market is pricing GLP-1 competition and US pricing pressure as permanent franchise impairment, just as the first-and-only oral GLP-1 for obesity (Wegovy pill, launched Jan 2026) begins to ramp ahead of expectations.
- **Key numbers:** Share price ~$44.70 (June 1, 2026), down ~39% YoY; forward P/E ~13.6-14x vs ~27x 10-yr average; FY26 guidance narrowed to sales/operating profit decline of 4-12% at constant FX; Novo Nordisk Foundation voting control ~77% (long-standing structure, exact 2026 % unverified)
- **Catalyst:** Oral Wegovy prescription ramp through H2 2026 (already exceeding initial expectations per Q1 report), guidance stabilization/return to growth in FY27, and resolution of US pricing framework removing the overhang.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Lilly's orforglipron and next-gen pipeline take dominant oral share; GLP-1 pricing deflates faster than volume grows (guidance already embeds double-digit profit decline); pipeline (CagriSema and successors) fails to keep Novo competitive — in which case this is a melting franchise, not a temporary discount.
- **Next steps:** Read the Q1 2026 6-K and August H1 report for oral Wegovy scripts and US net pricing; model a bear case where US GLP-1 net prices fall 40% and check if the current price is covered by diabetes + international obesity alone; track weekly IQVIA/analyst script data for oral vs injectable mix; verify current foundation ownership and buyback activity in the latest 20-F/annual report.

<a id="4-small-micro-cap-neglect"></a>

## 4. Small & Micro-Cap Neglect

**Why the edge exists:** The alpha is structural: roughly 28% of microcaps have zero sell-side coverage (vs ~4% of small caps), institutional funds cannot build positions in $100-500M illiquid names, and passive flows mechanically concentrate capital in mega-caps — the top 10 stocks are now ~39% of the S&P 500 and the small-cap trailing P/E discount to large caps (~37%, S&P 600 at ~15.6x forward vs S&P 500 at ~22-27x) is the widest in about two decades, so the setup IS at an extreme even after small caps outperformed in early 2026 (Russell 2000 +9.2% vs S&P 500 +4.1% through late April). Behavioral reinforcement: family/founder control (dual-class, 40-60% insider stakes) fails governance screens and keeps floats tiny, and the businesses are boring (heating oil, groceries, specialty insurance, land). The proof the discount is real is that it is being harvested by acquirers rather than by public investors: in this exact niche in the past 8 months FONAR was taken private ($19/sh, June 2026), Nathan's Famous agreed to a Smithfield takeover ($102/sh), Crawford United agreed to sell to SPX (~$83/sh), and Solitron announced a sale exploration — North American take-private activity rose >60% YoY in 2025 and PE dry powder keeps growing. The persistent risk transfer is: you get paid for illiquidity, no coverage, and minority-shareholder status alongside a controlling family; the edge persists because none of those three frictions is going away.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Screen (stockanalysis.com, TIKR, or Koyfin): US/Canada/UK listed, market cap $50M-$1B, trailing or forward P/E < 10 (or EV/EBIT < 8), ROE > 12%, net debt/EBITDA < 1.5, insider ownership > 20%, sell-side analysts <= 2, positive FCF in 4 of last 5 years
- Watch SEC filing streams: SC 13E-3 (going-private — study the fairness opinions to learn what control buyers pay for these assets), SC 13D (activists in sub-$500M names), Form 4 cluster buying, and DEF 14A proxies to verify true beneficial ownership rather than screener insider fields
- Track the take-out tape as a feeder: each deal (FONR, NATH, CRAWA, SODI in 2025-26) reveals which niches strategics/PE are paying up for and frees up specialist-fund capital that must be recycled into the survivors — keep a list of the closest public comps to every announced deal
- Mine microcap specialist 13Fs and letters quarterly (WhaleWisdom): funds under ~$300M AUM focused on this space, plus MicroCapClub, Value Investors Club, and deep-value substacks for names that have already had first-pass diligence
- OTC Markets: screen the OTCQX/OTCQB and 'dark' company lists (LICT, Pardee Resources, Hanover Foods, Burnham Holdings type names) — request annual reports directly; the disclosure discount is largest here
- Calendar-driven forced selling: June Russell reconstitution deletions and December tax-loss selling in names down >30% with <$500k/day volume — pre-build buy lists in May and November
- Verify the multiple is real: recompute earnings ex one-timers (weather, land-sale timing, insurance reserve releases), check share count from the latest 10-Q cover page, and confirm insider % from the most recent proxy before grading any idea

<a id="5-hidden-assets-sum-of-parts"></a>

## 5. Hidden Assets & Sum-of-Parts

**Why the edge exists:** This alpha persists because screens and income-statement-driven investors cannot see it: land carried at 1930s-1980s cost, listed stakes equity-accounted or marked below market, and NOLs produce no EPS, so quant and passive flows systematically underprice them. Realization usually requires a control event, and the controlling families/founders (Bolloré, Japanese cross-holding networks, Korean chaebol) have historically had tax, control, and prestige reasons NOT to crystallize value, so discounts persist for years and most institutions won't wait. The edge in 2026 is that external forcing mechanisms have multiplied — TSE governance reform, Korea's Value-Up program, French courts, and a record activist cycle — so pairing a verified hidden asset with a live catalyst converts a value trap into a special situation. Finally, these are mostly mid/small caps or "boring" holdcos with thin sell-side coverage, so mispricings are not arbitraged quickly.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Screen for holdcos whose listed-security stakes exceed 100-120% of their own market cap (negative stub): compute stake value from public share counts in annual reports vs holdco cap; maintain a tracker for SK Square, Porsche SE, Heineken Holding, Odet/Bollore chain, Kyocera, Keisei Electric, TBS Holdings, Loews, Liberty entities
- Japan: screen TSE for companies trading below (net cash + listed securities at market + real estate book), then read the yukashoken hokokusho fixed-asset schedule for land at pre-1990 acquisition cost; monitor EDINET 5% large-shareholding filings by Dalton, 3D Investment Partners, Palliser, Murakami-affiliated funds, Oasis
- US: SEC full-text search (efts.sec.gov) for 'sum-of-the-parts', 'carried at historical cost', and 'net operating loss carryforward' in 13D letters and proxy fights; follow 13D/13G filings by Nitor, Land & Buildings, Ancora, Politan-style specialists
- Read 10-K property footnotes and (for REIT-like filers) Schedule III to find land/buildings whose gross book value dates decades back (Tejon, St. Joe, FRP Holdings, Alexander's, Dillard's, railroads-adjacent landowners)
- NOL screen: post-restructuring or post-loss equities where 10-K tax footnote shows federal NOLs > 50% of market cap and a Section 382 rights plan exists (signal management values them); verify annual 382 limitation math
- Track corporate events that force marks: announced real-estate carve-outs/auctions in Japan (Sapporo/KKR-PAG precedent, Fuji Media restructuring), Korea Value-Up disclosures on KIND, court-mandated tender situations (Vivendi/Bollore at Paris Court of Appeal), Liberty-style split-offs and their post-split discount windows
- Water/land rights: Colorado and California water court dockets plus 10-Ks of Pure Cycle, Limoneira, Cadiz; Florida land: Alico and St. Joe county land-transaction records to benchmark per-acre marks
- Overfunded pensions: screen 10-K pension footnotes for plan assets minus PBO > 15% of market cap (potential surplus monetization via annuitization or IBM-style contribution holiday)

<a id="6-special-situations-event-driven"></a>

## 6. Special Situations & Event-Driven

**Why the edge exists:** Merger-arb capital in 2026 is crowding into large, clean, signed deals because the lighter-touch US antitrust regime (resumed HSR early terminations, faster closings, few breaks) has compressed those spreads; what gets left behind are deals whose binding constraint is a binary, hard-to-handicap sovereign or political approval — Israel's golden share (ZIM), CFIUS on Saudi/Japanese/Chinese-linked acquirers (EA, DigitalBridge, Nathan's Famous, made worse by a 2026 partial government shutdown pausing statutory CFIUS clocks) — where institutional arbs cannot model the tail and simply pass. Separately, liquidations that voluntarily delist and deregister (Stratus) force out index funds and mandate-constrained holders with no natural buyer for a non-reporting stub, and odd-lot tender priority and spin-off when-issued flows are structurally capped in size, so no fund large enough to move prices bothers to compete. The alpha persists because it is capacity-constrained, career-risky (headline binary outcomes), and requires reading proxies and tender documents rather than running screens.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- EDGAR full-text search (efts.sec.gov) weekly for new SC TO-I / SC TO-I/A filings containing 'odd lots' — issuer self-tenders where holders of <100 shares are exempt from proration (model: Optimum Communications OPTU June 2026 tender at $2.50, ~47% proration for everyone except odd-lots). Also watch reverse splits with fractional-share cash-outs via 13E-3 filings (model: Tile Shop TTSH Dec 2025).
- EDGAR full-text search for PREM14A/DEFM14A containing 'plan of complete liquidation and dissolution' plus 8-Ks announcing liquidating distributions; cross-check InsideArbitrage and StockTitan liquidation trackers. Prioritize liquidations with near-term partial distributions that shrink the capital at risk (STRS, AIV pattern).
- Weekly pass through InsideArbitrage's Merger Arbitrage Tool and Special Situation Investments' merger-arb tracker; filter for signed definitive agreements (exclude non-binding/unsolicited proposals) with gross spreads >6-8%, committed financing, and identify WHY the spread is wide — sovereign/foreign approval spreads are systematically overpriced for risk in 2026 vs. US antitrust spreads.
- Maintain a CFIUS-exposure list: any pending deal with a Saudi (PIF), Japanese (SoftBank), Chinese (WH Group/Smithfield), or Gulf acquirer of a US target. Track DPA filing dates and the 2026 government-shutdown impact on statutory review clocks; buy weakness caused by process delays (timeline risk) rather than substantive national-security objections.
- Monitor FTC second requests and HSR early-termination grants monthly to calibrate the antitrust regime: in the current administration a second request (e.g., Cintas/UniFirst, June 11, 2026) signals timeline extension far more often than litigation — spreads that gap on second-request headlines are candidates.
- Spin-off calendar (stockspinoffs.com, InsideArbitrage spin-off tracker): trade when-issued vs. regular-way discrepancies and post-distribution forced selling. Live pipeline: Middleby/Midera Food Processing (distributed July 6, 2026), Resideo/ADI Global Distribution (record July 20, distribution Aug 3, 2026, 'ADIG WI'), S&P Global/Mobility Global MBGL (distributed July 1, 2026). Check index-membership changes for mechanical sellers.
- EDGAR full-text search for 'oversubscription privilege' in S-1/S-3/424B filings: small-cap rights offerings where a control holder backstops and apathetic retail leaves the oversubscription pool underused (model: M-tron MPTI rights offering completed April 2026). Enter only when rights price is below conservative NAV/value.
- Track competing-bid / bumpitrage situations: targets trading ABOVE the signed deal price with a live interloper (Two Harbors pattern), repeated special-meeting adjournments, and ISS/Glass Lewis 'vote against' recommendations — adjournments are the tell that the board lacks votes and a bump or topping bid is in play.

<a id="7-holding-company-discounts-parent-sub-arbitrage"></a>

## 7. Holding-Company Discounts & Parent-Sub Arbitrage

**Why the edge exists:** Holdco discounts persist because there is no natural closing mechanism: controlling families cannot be displaced, so the gap only closes when the controller voluntarily acts (buyback, simplification, buyout) or is forced (courts, regulation, succession taxes). Institutions structurally avoid these vehicles — index providers penalize low float, sell-side coverage is thin because holdcos generate no banking fees, and multi-asset NAVs don't fit sector-analyst silos — leaving them owned by nobody's mandate. The behavioral edge in mid-2026 is regime change that is slowly priced: Korea's March 2026 Commercial Code amendment forces treasury-share cancellation, French courts are litigating whether Bolloré must buy out Vivendi minorities, and buyback math at 50%+ discounts (each euro retired buys two euros of assets) has finally converted controllers from beneficiaries of wide discounts into beneficiaries of narrow ones. Most investors still extrapolate 'the discount is permanent' from the old regime, so event-path patience is paid.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Build a look-through NAV sheet: ~80 global holdcos where listed stakes are marked daily; flag any name where market cap < 0.65x value of LISTED stakes alone (ignore unlisted). Company NAV pages to anchor: exor.com/pages/investors-media/net-asset-value-nav, gbl.com/en/net-asset-value, prosus.com (daily NAV), wendelgroup quarterly NAV, group.softbank/en/ir/stock/sotp.
- Buyback-at-discount screen: holdcos repurchasing >3% of shares/year while trading at >40% NAV discount (mechanical NAV/share accretion ~= discount x % retired). Watch Euronext/SGX daily buyback disclosures (Exor, GBL, Wendel, Jardine Matheson, Prosus).
- Korea catalyst monitor: DART filings for treasury-share cancellation resolutions after the Commercial Code amendment (effective 2026-03-06: new treasury shares cancelled within 1 year, existing within 18 months); KRX Value-Up disclosure list; holdcos with >50% discounts (Samsung C&T, LG Corp, SK Inc, CJ, Hanwha) plus activist letters (Palliser, Whitebox, FCP, Align Partners).
- France/AMF monitor: AMF decision feed (amf-france.org) for mandatory-offer and squeeze-out rulings in the Bollore/Vivendi complex; Cour de Cassation and Paris Court of Appeal calendars; any ruling that treasury shares count toward the 30% mandatory-offer threshold rereads across every French controlled holdco (Odet, Bollore, Peugeot Invest, Lagardere/Louis Hachette).
- Japan parent-child unwind list: TSE's disclosure push on parent-subsidiary listings; screen listed subs >50% owned by listed parents and parents below stake value (Toyota Industries closed March 2026 at a 25%+ bump from first offer after Elliott pressure - the template repeats). Watch TOB filings on EDINET and Nikkei M&A flags.
- Activist 13D/requisition tracker on holdco targets: Palliser (Rio Tinto DLC, Samsung C&T), Elliott (SoftBank, Toyota complex), Whitebox; AGM requisitioned resolutions at DLCs - a >20% vote for review under UK rules forces board response (Palliser's Rio resolution got 19.35% in 2025, refiled 2026).
- Dual-class/DLC spread z-scores: daily series on Heineken Holding vs Heineken, Christian Dior vs look-through LVMH, Rio Tinto plc vs Ltd, Lennar A vs B, Swatch bearer vs registered; investigate anything >2 standard deviations from 5-year mean, but require a governance catalyst before sizing.
- Succession screen: controlling patriarch >80 years old or announced handover (Bollore family, Korean chaebol third-generation inheritance-tax events) - estate taxes force stake sales, collapses of cross-holdings, or take-privates; read local-language press, not just filings.

**Candidates:**

> **⚠️ ADVERSE RULING 2026-07-08:** the Paris Court of Appeal ruled Bolloré does NOT control Vivendi. The realized event outcome does not validate the original probability estimates or illustrative 2-3% sizing, and no executed position or realized portfolio return is documented. The revised thesis, numbers and next steps are in the [Domain 5 entry](#5-hidden-assets-sum-of-parts).

  - **Catalyst:** Paris Court of Appeal re-hearing (argued May 22, 2026; advocate general already opined Bollore controls Vivendi); Vivendi appealed to Cour de Cassation and lodged a CJEU appeal June 9, 2026 - a final adverse ruling forces an AMF-supervised buyout offer for minorities.
  - **Kill criteria / risks:** Courts rule no de facto control (Cour de Cassation already vacated the April 2025 ruling once, Nov 28, 2025); multi-year legal limbo with no interim catalyst; Bollore engineers an offer at an unfavorable price; UMG share-price decline shrinks the NAV while you wait.
  - **Next steps:** Read the AMF Nov 13, 2024 decision, the Cour de Cassation Nov 28, 2025 ruling, and the 2026 Court of Appeal decision plus Vivendi's June 9, 2026 CJEU release (businesswire/vivendi.com); rebuild NAV per share line-by-line (UMG shares held x price, net cash post-TIM sale, treasury shares); model buyout price scenarios under AMF fair-price ('multi-criteria') methodology; check who else is in the stock (CIAM and other minority activists) and the free-float size.
- **Status:** demoted to C 2026-07-13 — mandatory-tender catalyst dead; cassation-appeal lottery + SOTP only

### [A] Exor N.V. — `EXO (Euronext Amsterdam)`
- **Thesis:** Agnelli family holdco at a ~55-62% discount to a published NAV of EUR 164.4/share, run by a management team explicitly buying back stock at half of NAV (EUR 1bn completed) with EUR 3.7bn of 2026 firepower - the widest discount among large-cap Western holdcos with the cleanest balance sheet.
- **Key numbers:** NAV/share EUR 164.4 at end-2025, GAV EUR 37.1bn vs market cap ~EUR 17bn (verified via company release 2026-03-23); discount reported 57-62% (verified as broker/press range); LTV 6.9% (verified); EUR 1bn buyback executed at ~half NAV adding ~EUR 4.7/share to NAV (verified); ~EUR 3.7bn deployable cash in 2026 (verified from earnings call coverage).
- **Catalyst:** Continuation/expansion of buybacks at extreme accretion; Ferrari shareholder pact extended with Piero Ferrari (Jan 2026) securing control with a smaller stake, enabling further Ferrari trims funding buybacks; possible large disposal (CNH/Iveco proceeds) recycled into repurchases.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Ferrari is ~50% of NAV - a Ferrari derating (already underway since late 2025) drops NAV faster than buybacks accrete; Agnelli family (via Giovanni Agnelli BV) will never sell control so the discount has no hard floor; management redeploys cash into private assets (Lingotto, healthcare) the market marks at zero.
- **Next steps:** Read the FY2025 annual report and March 2026 NAV presentation on exor.com; decompose NAV into listed (Ferrari, Stellantis, CNH, Philips) vs unlisted (Lingotto, ventures) and compute the discount on listed-only assets; model buyback accretion at various discount levels vs a Ferrari bear case; check Dutch withholding/tax friction on any future capital returns.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [A] Samsung C&T — `028260 (KRX)`
- **Thesis:** De facto holdco of the Samsung group (stakes in Samsung Electronics and Samsung Biologics) at a ~63% NAV discount, now forced by Korea's amended Commercial Code and its own commitment to cancel the entire 13.2% treasury block - the single largest mechanical catalyst in the Korea value-up complex.
- **Key numbers:** ~63% discount to NAV (verified via Douglas Research, single source - treat as approximate); cancelled 4.6% of common treasury shares in Q1 2026 (verified); committed to retire all 13.2% treasury holding within 5 years, now backstopped by the Commercial Code amendment effective 2026-03-06 mandating cancellation (verified); Samsung group-wide ~USD 14bn treasury cancellations announced March 2026 (verified via Bloomberg).
- **Catalyst:** Mandatory treasury-share cancellation under the March 2026 Commercial Code; KRX Value-Up disclosures and the government's 'Kospi 5000' program; continued activist presence (Palliser, Whitebox previously at 63%-undervaluation thesis); possible group restructuring around Lee-family inheritance-tax funding.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Discount is partly structural (circular ownership, chance C&T is used as the family's control vehicle in a value-destructive restructuring); Samsung Electronics price drives NAV; cancellation of treasury shares alone returns no cash - thesis needs dividends/buybacks on top; Korean regulatory momentum could stall after elections.
- **Next steps:** Pull current price and recompute NAV: listed stakes (Samsung Electronics ~5%, Samsung Biologics ~43%, Samsung SDS, Samsung E&A) vs market cap from DART filings; read the 2026 value-up disclosure and treasury cancellation schedule; track quarterly cancellation execution on KRX; review Palliser/Whitebox letters for the governance ask-list and check what has actually been adopted.
- **Status:** demoted to watch 2026-07-02 — deep dive ([ideas/korea-value-up.md](ideas/korea-value-up.md)) found the cancellation catalyst largely priced after a 135-205% run, and the KOSPI unwind (-18.5% from June 19 peak, won at 17-year low) is live. Revisit after the panic stabilizes.

- **Thesis:** Pure parent-below-listed-stakes situation: Porsche SE trades at a ~37% discount to the market value of its VW and Porsche AG stakes, with the discount having widened from 28% since January 2026 - you buy VW/Porsche AG exposure at two-thirds price with the Porsche-Piech family aligned via dividends.
- **Key numbers:** Holdco discount widened from 28% (Jan 2026) to 37%, vs 12-month average 30% (verified, BofA estimate via press); Q1 2026 net loss EUR 923m on a EUR 1.3bn VW stake writedown (verified); owns 31.9% of VW capital / majority of ordinaries plus 12.5% direct Porsche AG stake (stake sizes unverified this cycle - confirm in filings); net debt at holdco level unverified.
- **Catalyst:** VW cash returns flowing up (dividend); announced diversification into defense/infrastructure could force clearer capital-allocation communication; any simplification of the double-decker VW/Porsche AG structure; German auto cycle recovery mechanically narrows the writedown-driven pessimism.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Double-holdco discount is chronic - family control, no buyback culture, and residual diesel-era litigation; a structural VW/Porsche AG reshuffle could be done at minorities' expense; German auto secular decline makes the NAV itself a melting ice cube; discount can sit at 35-40% for years without catalyst.
- **Next steps:** Read Porsche SE's Q1 2026 interim statement and NAV disclosure (porsche-se.com); recompute stake values at current VW ord/pref and Porsche AG prices vs market cap and net holdco debt; check litigation reserve status; assess whether the defense-investment strategy dilutes the pure-play stake story or adds a re-rating narrative.

- **Thesis:** Same economic entity, two prices: plc has persistently traded well below Ltd, and Palliser Capital keeps requisitioning unification resolutions (19.35% support in 2025, refiled at the May 6, 2026 AGM) - buy plc, optionally short Ltd, and get paid if BHP-style unification ever happens.
- **Key numbers:** 2025 AGM vote: 19.35% for the DLC review, just under the 20% UK threshold forcing wider consultation (verified); 2026 AGM held May 6, 2026 with the resolution refiled and board again opposed (verified); current plc-vs-Ltd spread unverified (historically ~15-25% - must measure); Palliser estimates of unification value unverified.

- **Kill criteria / risks:** Board has rejected unification twice with five external advisers citing multi-billion tax cost - catalyst may simply never arrive; the spread can widen further; a short-Ltd hedge carries franking-credit and borrow costs; this is a years-long carry trade, not an event trade.
- **Next steps:** Measure the live plc/Ltd spread and its 5-year z-score; read Rio's 2026 AGM statement on the DLC resolution and the 2026 vote tally (riotinto.com RNS); read Palliser's unification white paper; quantify the franking-credit drag that justifies part of the spread to isolate the truly anomalous portion.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

<a id="8-japan-korea-governance-reform-value"></a>

## 8. Japan & Korea Governance-Reform Value

**Why the edge exists:** The alpha comes from a slow-motion forced transfer of assets from non-economic holders to economic ones: cross-shareholders, parents, and entrenched boards are being compelled by regulators (TSE's cost-of-capital directive and the ~July 2026 CG Code revision with its explicit cash/real-estate utilization principle; Korea's 2025-26 Commercial Code amendments including the March 2026 mandatory treasury-share cancellation) to release capital on pre-announced timetables that most investors still discount as talk. Domestic institutions were historically passive and foreign coverage of sub-$3bn Japanese cash-boxes and Korean preferred lines is thin, so mechanical catalysts (a dated 20% share cancellation, a tender for a listed subsidiary, an AGM vote on retaining treasury stock) sit in public filings unpriced. In Korea specifically, the 2026 melt-up itself created fresh dislocation: index/ETF and momentum flows chase common shares, so preferred-share disparities WIDENED to >60% even as governance mechanically improved — a behavioral, flow-driven mispricing. The edge persists because realization takes 1-3 years per name, punishing benchmark-hugging capital and rewarding concentrated, patient balance-sheet-reading investors — exactly the Buffett template already validated by Berkshire taking all five trading houses above 10%.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Japan screen: TSE Prime/Standard, P/B < 1, (net cash + listed securities + investment real estate) > 50% of market cap, ROE < 8% — cross-reference against JPX's monthly-updated list of companies that have NOT yet disclosed 'cost of capital and stock price conscious management' plans (jpx.co.jp follow-up page); non-disclosers face escalating naming pressure under the July 2026 revised CG Code.
- EDINET large-shareholding (5%) reports: new or increased filings by City Index Eleventh / Reno / Aya Nomura (Murakami vehicles), Dalton, Oasis, Elliott, Palliser, 3D Investment Partners, Farallon — Japan set a record 56 campaigns in 2025 and 2026 is running hotter; buy the second wave, not the pop (e.g., Mitsui Matsushima Holdings 1518 JP, where City Index's joint stake is 27%).
- Parent-child pipeline: JPX's list of listed subsidiaries and equity-method affiliates with parent stakes >40%; watch for 'special committee established' announcements (the tell before a tender) — the Toyota Industries (¥6.7tn, closed Mar 2026, Elliott forced a bump) and NTT Data precedents set the premium template; TSE has said it will publish investor perspectives on parent-subsidiary listings and consider listing rules.
- Cross-shareholding unwind: the three megabanks and P&C insurers (Tokio Marine, MS&AD, Sompo) committed to full policy-shareholding exit within ~6 years — mine the 'policy shareholdings' table in each yukashoken hokokusho for companies whose top-10 register is dominated by these forced sellers; the overhang removal usually comes packaged with a buyback.
- Korea treasury-stock screen: DART filings for 'Treasury Share Retention and Disposal Plans' (now requiring annual shareholder approval); screen KOSPI/KOSDAQ for treasury >10% of shares outstanding — cancellation is now mandated within 1 year for new purchases / 18-month grace for existing (law effective Mar 6, 2026; KRW 15.8tn cancelled in March 2026 alone, ~$42bn total pipeline estimated); pre-reform heavies: Lotte Corp 27.4%, SK Inc 24.8%, HD Hyundai 10.5%.
- Korea preferred-share disparity screen: same-issuer pref/common disparity >50% with pari-passu-plus dividends and ADV > $1m — the June 2026 Seoul Economic Daily data shows blue-chip disparities at multi-year wides (Hyundai Motor >60%, LG Electronics high-60s%) precisely because ETF flows buy common only.
- Korea holdco NAV screen: holding companies at >55% discount to spot NAV where a dated cancellation event exists (SK Inc template); track KRX Value-Up disclosure tracker and the Feb 2026 corporate-tax amendment requiring Value-Up plans for dividend tax benefits — filers are self-selecting reformers.
- Berkshire replication watch: Berkshire crossed 10% on all five shosha in May 2026 (filings via Kanto Local Finance Bureau); monitor for down-tier moves and screen next-tier trading/industrial conglomerates (Sojitz 2768, Kanematsu 8020, Toyota Tsusho 8015, Iwatani 8088) on P/B vs 10-year ROE.
- Event calendar to maintain: July 2026 revised CG Code effective date; July 2027 governance-report compliance deadline; Jan 4, 2027 SK Inc cancellation; Sept 2026 Korea existing-treasury grace-period expiry (18 months from Mar 2026); June AGM season vote results on activist proposals (Keisei, Fuji Media).

**Candidates:**

### [A] SK Inc. (SK Group holding company) — `034730 KS (KRX)`
- **Thesis:** Korean holdco trading at a wide NAV discount into a legally-forced capital event: Korea's March 2026 treasury-cancellation mandate compelled SK to announce cancellation of 20.1% of all issued shares (14,694,388 shares, effective Jan 4, 2027), while its SK hynix stake NAV has exploded during the AI memory boom.
- **Key numbers:** Treasury held ~24.8% of shares pre-reform; 20.1% of issued shares to be cancelled Jan 4, 2027 (verified, board resolution Mar 10, 2026, ~$3.6bn); analyst-cited NAV/share ~KRW 950,000 vs market price at a 'considerable gap' (discount level as of July 2026 unverified); stock +16% single day in June 2026 on hynix spillover.
- **Catalyst:** Jan 4, 2027 cancellation date; annual shareholder approval now required to RETAIN remaining treasury shares; continued SK hynix earnings; possible further Value-Up disclosure tied to Feb 2026 corporate tax incentives.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** KOSPI has already roughly tripled since mid-2025 — a market-wide de-rating hits holdcos hardest; SK hynix memory-cycle rollover collapses NAV; chaebol re-leveraging or NAV-destructive rescue of weak affiliates (SK On/battery); discount is structural and may persist post-cancellation.
- **Next steps:** Pull DART filings: Mar 10, 2026 board resolution and the AGM-approved Treasury Share Retention and Disposal Plan; rebuild NAV bridge (hynix stake, SK Telecom, SK Innovation, SK E&S, unlisted assets) at current prices; compute pro-forma discount post-cancellation; check holdco net debt and any battery-affiliate support commitments; compare vs Lotte Corp (27.4% treasury but distressed chemicals) as the same trade with worse assets.
- **Status:** demoted to watch 2026-07-02 — deep dive ([ideas/korea-value-up.md](ideas/korea-value-up.md)) judged the Jan 4, 2027 cancellation already priced after the run; KOSPI unwind live. Better re-entry likely into the panic — recheck NAV discount weekly.

### [A] Hyundai Motor preferred shares (1P/2PB/3PB) vs common — `005387 / 005385 / 005389 KS (KRX)`
- **Thesis:** The pref-common disparity WIDENED from ~30% to >60% during the 2026 KOSPI melt-up because index/ETF and momentum flows chase common shares only — you get the same dividend stream (prefs typically +small extra dividend) at roughly half price, with Value-Up buyback/cancellation mechanics (companies must now cancel repurchased shares) disproportionately favoring cheap share classes.
- **Key numbers:** Disparity ratio on Hyundai's three pref lines rose from ~30% (Jan 2026) to >60% (June 2026, Seoul Economic Daily — verified); common +130% YTD 2026 vs prefs +20-30% (verified); Hyundai announced KRW 400bn buyback-and-cancel in 2026 (verified); pref dividend yield at current prices unverified.
- **Catalyst:** Mandatory cancellation of repurchased shares (effective Mar 6, 2026) makes pref buybacks the cheapest EPS/DPS accretion; Feb 2026 tax-law change pushes high-dividend payers to file Value-Up plans; mean-reversion of a spread at multi-year wides; same trade exists in LG Electronics prefs (high-60s% disparity) and Samsung Electronics pref (KRW 184,400, June 2026).
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Spread is a flow phenomenon and can stay wide or widen if the common-share mania continues; no voting rights means no control premium ever; a KOSPI crash drops prefs in absolute terms even if spread narrows; company may buy back only common.
- **Next steps:** Build the disparity time series for Hyundai 1P/2PB/3PB, LG Electronics pref, Samsung Electronics pref vs 10-year history; read Hyundai's shareholder-return policy and 2026 buyback disclosures to see which share class is repurchased; check pref liquidity (ADV) for position sizing; model total return under spread narrowing to the historical 30-40% band vs staying at 60%.
- **Status:** **conditional PURSUE — trigger ₩175,000 still working (not hit).** 07-13 update: lowest print of the episode is TODAY (₩198,500 low / ₩199,900 close, -2.0%) even as KOSPI rebounded to ~7,476 and the won recovered to ~1,501-1,511 — prefs weakening on target cuts, not panic; ~12% above trigger. Note: the index panic low was Jul 8 (~7,247), not Jul 2. See also the 2026-07-13 sweep inside domain 11 (Samsung pref 005935, Kia) for stronger-graded panic legs.

### [A] Keisei Electric Railway (stub trade on Oriental Land stake) — `9009 JP (TSE Prime)`
- **Thesis:** Classic parent-affiliate discount: Keisei's ~21.5% stake in Oriental Land (Tokyo Disney) has at times exceeded Keisei's own market cap, and Palliser Capital (4.4%) is running an escalating campaign — including a formal vote-no against the CEO/chair — demanding the stake be cut and proceeds returned, exactly the situation the July 2026 CG Code revision (effective-use-of-assets principle) targets.
- **Key numbers:** Keisei holds ~21.5% of Oriental Land (verified); Palliser stake 4.4% (verified); Palliser demanded reduction to <15% of OLC by end-March 2026 — compliance status unverified; implied stub value of the railway business and current stake-value-to-market-cap ratio unverified (must recompute at current prices).
- **Catalyst:** June 2026 AGM vote-no results and board response; the ~July 2026 revised CG Code explicitly requires boards to justify holding non-core financial assets; TSE's forthcoming rules on parent/affiliate listings; any OLC selldown triggers buybacks.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Management entrenchment — Palliser owns only 4.4% and prior proposals failed; OLC itself is expensive and a de-rating shrinks the asset; capital-gains tax leakage on any selldown; railway core business is low-ROE ballast.
- **Next steps:** Recompute the stub: current OLC market value x 21.5% vs Keisei EV; read Keisei's response to Palliser and the 2026 AGM voting results (check ISS/Glass Lewis recommendations); read Keisei's yukashoken hokokusho for the stated 'rationale' for the OLC holding; model per-share value under 15% / 10% / full exit scenarios with tax.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

<a id="9-uk-continental-europe-discount"></a>

## 9. UK & Continental Europe Discount

**Why the edge exists:** Two decades of structural de-equitization (UK pension funds cutting domestic equities to low single digits, MiFID II gutting small/mid-cap research, passive flows concentrating in US mega-caps) have left the FTSE All-Share at ~12.7x forward earnings vs ~22.5x for the Russell 1000 — a near-record ~35% Europe-vs-US multiple gap — even though the US-vs-Europe earnings growth differential is forecast to shrink to ~2 points over 2025-27. There is no natural marginal buyer of UK/European mid-caps, so the gap is being closed by corporates and acquirers instead: 28 UK takeovers announced by June 2026 (£39.3bn, 86% foreign acquirers, 45% average premium) and record cash returns (FTSE 100 buybacks tracking ~£58bn again in 2026, total cash return ~£117bn = 4.4% of index cap). Family holdcos and dual-share structures (D'Ieteren, Jungheinrich prefs) add a second discount layer because they screen badly and have low free float, so mispricing persists until a hard corporate event (IPO, minority-stake mark, activist campaign, firm offer) forces price discovery — which is exactly what the current bid wave keeps supplying. The persistence is behavioral (career risk of owning 'cheap for a reason' UK assets) and structural (flows), not informational, so patient fundamental buyers who underwrite standalone value and get the event for free keep getting paid.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Track live UK bid situations weekly: RNS Rule 2.4/2.7 announcements and Takeover Panel PUSU deadline table; the pattern in 2025-26 is bidders returning 3-4 times (Intertek: 4 EQT bids ending +62% over undisturbed; easyJet: 4+ Castlelake bids) — buy quality targets after the FIRST rejected approach, underwriting standalone value as the downside
- Screen the take-private profile that matched ~74% of 2025 firm offers: LSE-listed, market cap £150m-£2bn, EV/EBITDA < 8x, FCF yield > 8%, net debt/EBITDA < 1.5x, low analyst coverage (<4), meaningful US revenue; run monthly on FTSE 250/SmallCap/AIM
- Pair-trade style peer-gap screen: each LSE quality name vs its closest US comp (Rentokil vs Rollins, Melrose vs TransDigm/Heico, Smith & Nephew vs Stryker/Zimmer, BAT vs Philip Morris/Altria, Ashtead vs United Rentals); investigate when the forward multiple gap exceeds ~40% with comparable growth/ROIC
- Follow activist TR-1 disclosures (>3% and each 1% move) in UK names — Cevian, Trian, Palliser, Elliott — via LSE RNS holdings-in-company notifications; activist entry is the most reliable precursor to breakup/relisting/take-private events in this market
- Continental family holdco/dual-share screen: SOTP or NAV discounts > 40% WITH a dated hard catalyst — announced IPO of the main asset, a minority-stake transaction that marks NAV (e.g. Belron at €32.2bn EV), or a family reorganisation; also German preference shares trading at wide gaps to family-held ordinaries (Jungheinrich, Henkel, Sixt)
- Buyback cannibal screen: FTSE 350 companies retiring >5% of share count annually at single-digit FCF multiples — use AJ Bell's quarterly Dividend Dashboard/buyback tracker (2026 YTD: £34.6bn announced, on pace to match 2025's record £58.6bn)
- Monitor closing-the-gap policy flow: Pension Schemes Act 2026 implementation and the (limited) mandation power, Mansion House Accord allocations, any UK ISA/stamp-duty reform consultations — these are FTSE 250/small-cap flow catalysts; also track relisting announcements (primary-listing moves to NYSE) as both a risk to LSE holdings and a re-rating catalyst for the movers
- Read every UK Takeover Panel Rule 2.9 announcement and scheme document for the acquirer's stated valuation logic — PE bidders' paid multiples (Intertek ~£10.9bn equity, 40%+ premiums) are the best live comps for the remaining listed names in the same niche (testing/inspection/certification, compliance software, specialty distribution)

<a id="10-cyclical-troughs-capital-cycle-bottoms"></a>

## 10. Cyclical Troughs (Capital-Cycle Bottoms)

**Why the edge exists:** Trough cyclicals screen terribly on the metrics quant/momentum capital uses: trailing P/E is sky-high on collapsed EPS (or the company is loss-making), estimate revisions are still negative, and price momentum is falling — so systematic strategies, index-relative benchmarkers, and career-risk-averse PMs mechanically avoid them until the "all-clear" earnings inflection, by which point the re-rate has happened. The durable edge is that the market fixates on weak current DEMAND headlines and under-weights the slow-moving SUPPLY response (capacity closures, orderbook-to-fleet, capex-below-depreciation) that actually sets up the next up-cycle — the Marathon/Chancellor capital-cycle insight. Passive flows don't distinguish trough from peak, and the same behavioral reflex causes the mirror error: crowding into things that merely look cheap after a run (memory today) or that are at record profitability (tankers) while calling them "cyclical." The alpha is doing the supply-side work and having the stomach to buy a falling knife with a clean-enough balance sheet.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Screen for the trough signature: HIGH trailing EV/EBITDA but LOW EV/mid-cycle-EBITDA, plus cheap EV/sales, EV/gross PP&E and price/book — depressed or negative current earnings hide normalized value.
- Track physical capacity exit, not demand: ICIS / C&EN / Wood Mackenzie plant-closure trackers (chemicals — ~37Mt European capacity closing, 16% of steam-cracking); Clarksons orderbook-to-fleet and scrapping (shipping); rig count + cold-stacked fleet (offshore); housing starts vs. household formation and LIRA remodeling index (building products).
- Confirm capex starvation: industry-wide capex/D&A below 1.0x for multiple years is the pre-condition for a supply-driven recovery.
- Read the filings for supply language: 10-K/10-Q MD&A and earnings calls mentioning 'footprint optimization,' 'reliability,' 'cost curve,' capacity rationalization, and segment margins vs. 10-year history.
- Contrarian entry signals: a dividend cut that DE-RISKS the balance sheet (LYB), buybacks/insider buying executed during reported losses, founder/family-controlled operators, and the FIRST sequential (QoQ constant-currency) revenue inflection after a multi-year decline (RHI).
- Screen OUT mislabeled troughs: reject anything where current pricing is at/near record highs — memory DRAM/NAND is in a +50-100% QoQ supercycle and tankers are at 2008/09 peak earnings; these are cycle tops dressed as cyclicals.
- Screen OUT value traps: structural (not cyclical) cost disadvantage (European commodity chemicals' energy penalty), secular demand loss (AI insourcing vs. staffing; acetate tow), or incoming greenfield supply that caps the recovery (potash / BHP Jansen ramp).
- Identify and study the best-in-class low-cost survivor in each troughed industry — the operator that gains share and pricing as high-cost capacity exits (US Gulf Coast integrated chemicals vs. European; scale distributors vs. regional).

**Candidates:**

### [A] LyondellBasell Industries — `LYB (NYSE)`
- **Thesis:** Low-cost, US-integrated polyethylene/olefins producer at the bottom of the petrochemical capital cycle; ~37Mt of mostly high-cost European capacity is physically exiting (16% of steam-cracking), tightening global supply and lifting margins for survivors, while a completed dividend cut has de-risked the balance sheet.
- **Key numbers:** ~$74/sh (Apr 2026 print; current unverified), forward P/E ~6x, dividend cut to $0.69/qtr ($2.76/yr, ~4.9% yield), Q1'26 adj EPS $0.49 beat $0.31; O&P-Americas EBITDA roughly doubled QoQ.
- **Catalyst:** European cracker/plant closures removing supply through 2026-27; polyethylene margin normalization off trough; Middle East disruption tightening PE prices.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Chinese petchem overcapacity offsets European exits; demand-led recovery slips to 2027+; naphtha/ethane spread compression; risk of a further capital-return reset.
- **Next steps:** Read Q1'26 10-Q MD&A and cash-flow statement; model normalized integrated-PE margins vs. current; overlay the ICIS European closure tracker onto the global cost curve; verify net debt, maturity wall and buyback capacity post-dividend cut.

### [C] Nutrien — `NTR (NYSE/TSX)`
- **Thesis:** Best-in-class integrated fertilizer producer plus ag-retail franchise with potash at a cyclical price trough and record volumes; watchlist-quality because structural oversupply (BHP Jansen ramping toward marginal cost) caps the potash recovery — own only at deeper value or via the more-defensible retail/nitrogen mix.
- **Key numbers:** 2025 FCF ~$2.0B, adj EBITDA ~$6.05B, retail EBITDA guide $1.75-1.95B, potash volume guide 14.1-14.8Mt (record); MOP ~$380/t; trailing P/E ~35x on trough EPS (unverified as spot); analyst target ~C$85.
- **Catalyst:** Nitrogen price surge (Middle East gas disruption) lifting near-term earnings; record potash volumes; structural retail-segment growth and rising cash returns.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Jansen + brownfield expansions push potash toward mid-$300s/t marginal cost; nitrogen surge reverses if geopolitics ease; not statistically cheap on current P/E — value is in normalized/SOTP, not the screen.
- **Next steps:** Build a SOTP valuing ag-retail separately from potash-at-marginal-cost; track the BHP Jansen ramp schedule; compare to Mosaic (MOS, pure potash/phosphate, no nitrogen) and CF Industries (CF, pure nitrogen, ~10.5x fwd, ~12% FCF yield, active buyback) to pick the cleanest expression.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

<a id="11-forced-non-economic-selling"></a>

## 11. Forced & Non-Economic Selling

**Why the edge exists:** The counterparty is not expressing a view on value: index trackers must sell deletions to minimize tracking error, IG mandates must dump bonds the day they become junk, wind-down trusts must return capital, and ethics committees must divest regardless of price. The effect is documented — Research Affiliates finds S&P 500 deletions outperform by ~5%/yr for five years, and fallen-angel studies show ~9% average price decline in the five weeks post-downgrade followed by recovery. The edge persists because the buyers who could absorb it face the same constraints (benchmark/career risk in owning demoted, headline-ugly names), the affected securities are small relative to institutional size, and 2026's shift to semi-annual Russell reconstitutions plus record passive share keeps enlarging the mechanical flow. Capacity is limited and the names are temporarily orphaned by sell-side coverage, so the mispricing refills every rebalance cycle.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- S&P DJI index announcements (spglobal.com/spdji index news, typically ~2 weeks pre-effective) and quarterly rebalances (Mar/Jun/Sep/Dec): screen each deletion for (a) removal due to market-cap drift only, not M&A or distress; (b) positive TTM earnings and guidance intact; (c) net debt/EBITDA < 3x. Buy after the effective-date volume spike, not the announcement.
- FTSE Russell reconstitution preliminary lists (published ~May 22 for June; NEW: second semi-annual recon in Nov 2026) — pull the official adds/deletes PDF from lseg.com; focus on Russell 1000->2000 demotions and growth-index style deletions where value-index buying is smaller than growth-index selling (e.g., NCLH June 2026).
- Forward pipeline for S&P deletions: track the 3-5 smallest S&P 500 members by float cap (CPB was flagged months ahead); Kalshi prediction markets on S&P adds/removes give a live consensus.
- Fallen angels: rating-agency feeds for BBB-/Baa3 on negative watch (JPMorgan estimates ~$84bn of fallen angels in 2026); buy bonds 4-6 weeks post-downgrade after IG-index exit; monitor FALN/ANGL ETF holdings changes as the forced-flow footprint.
- UK/European investment trusts: AIC sector discount data + QuotedData/Portfolio Adviser wind-down and continuation-vote announcements. Screen: managed wind-down announced, share price discount > 30%, and recent asset disposals completed within ~10% of carrying NAV (proves the marks).
- Private credit stress: track non-traded BDC/interval-fund redemption gates (BlackRock HPS gated withdrawals at its 5% quarterly cap in 2026) and buy quality LISTED BDCs sold in sympathy — screen for > 20% discount to NAV with non-accruals < 2% and senior-first-lien-heavy books.
- ESG/ethics exclusions: NBIM exclusion list, PFZW/ABP/AkademikerPension announcements; note Norway PAUSED new exclusions pending its ethics-framework review due Oct 15, 2026 — re-inclusion decisions are a buyable reversal flow.
- Country-risk dumping: after macro shocks (e.g., the March 2026 Iran/Strait of Hormuz crisis), rank single-country ETF drawdowns vs. constituents' foreign-revenue share; buy domestic earners sold indiscriminately. Also 13F/N-PORT filings of announced fund liquidations to front-run the last forced prints.

**Candidates:**

### [A] Pool Corporation — `POOL (Nasdaq)`
- **Thesis:** Dominant US pool-supplies distributor demoted from S&P 500 straight to SmallCap 600 (effective June 22, 2026) purely on market-cap drift after a ~40% one-year drawdown; the installed-base maintenance revenue is intact and the seller (S&P 500 trackers) is price-insensitive.
- **Key numbers:** FY2026 EPS guidance $10.87-$11.17 confirmed April 2026; Q1 2026 net sales +6%, operating income +7%; stock down ~40% over past year at removal; current price and forward P/E unverified (analyst PTs span Stifel $210 to Oppenheimer $280); insider stake unverified.
- **Catalyst:** Rebalance flow completed June 22, 2026 (S&P 500-tracked assets dwarf SmallCap 600 buying); new-pool construction cyclical trough; SmallCap 600 inclusion brings small-cap value buyers over following quarters.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Housing/discretionary downturn deepens (new construction is the margin kicker); maintenance-vs-discretionary revenue mix worse than assumed; permanent share loss to online/direct channels; multiple was historically rich so 'cheap' must be verified against guidance, not history.
- **Next steps:** Read Q1 2026 10-Q and Feb 2026 FY25 10-K (SEC 8-K exhibit pool-ex99_1.htm); split revenue maintenance vs. new construction; model trough EPS if construction falls another 20%; pull closing volume on June 19-22 to size the forced flow; check buyback pace and insider purchases post-demotion.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

PURSUE small, slow money.

### ── 2026-07-13 sweep: July 2026 KOSPI panic / "Meta shock" (LIVE, not yet stabilized) ──

**Event mechanics (why this alpha exists):** The KOSPI rose ~101% in H1 2026 on the AI-memory supercycle (Samsung +415% y/y at peak, hynix through ₩3mn), peaking at 9,114.55 on June 19. The unwind has five *mechanical, non-economic* sellers stacked on top of each other: (1) record foreign net selling — ₩148.3tn in H1 (₩44.7tn May, ₩48.6tn June), ₩157.3tn YTD through Jul 3, 13 straight sell sessions Jun 19–Jul 7, Samsung foreign ownership at a 16-yr low — driven by profit-taking + FX reflexivity (won at 17-yr low ~1,555 → FX losses → more selling → weaker won); (2) NPS rebalancing resumed July 1 with domestic equities ~30% vs 20.8% target — Shinyoung sizes forced supply at ₩14.7–74.4tn depending on index level; (3) program-selling cascades — 18 sell sidecars YTD incl. back-to-back Jul 7–8 and Jul 13; (4) retail leverage flush — single-stock leveraged ETFs on Samsung/hynix crushed below ₩20,000 listing prices; (5) ₩45.5tn of new hynix paper via the Jul 10 Nasdaq ADR listing. The pin was the **Meta shock**: Jul 1 Bloomberg report (confirmed) that Meta Compute will sell excess AI capacity (~500 MW) as a cloud business — the first hyperscaler admitting compute surplus — read as the top of the AI-capex cycle. Micron -11% (~$138bn mcap) Jul 1; KOSPI -7.89% Jul 2 (hynix -14.57%, Samsung -9.06%); -4.91% Jul 7; -5.35% Jul 8 to 7,246.79; two-day bounce Jul 9–10 (+0.62%, +2.52% to 7,475.94, foreigners briefly net buyers, won back to ~1,500) — **then broken on Jul 13 by an orthogonal shock**: US–Iran strikes and Tehran declaring Hormuz "closed" (Brent +3.9% to ~$79) sent KOSPI -5.6% intraday to ~7,060 (Samsung -10.5%, hynix -11%). Korea imports essentially all its crude seaborne, so leg two has a real macro channel — but index-level program selling is again hitting won-beneficiary exporters, net-cash value names, and record-discount prefs indiscriminately. The tape is bifurcated (banks +8%, defense +8% on Jul 2) except on sidecar days, when everything trades as one instrument. **Stabilization checklist before buying anything cyclical here:** USD/KRW <1,520 and holding a week; ≥5 consecutive foreign net-buy sessions (Jul 8–9 flip lasted 2 days — insufficient); 10 sessions without a sidecar; late-July hyperscaler Q2 capex guides intact (consensus $725bn 2026 big-four capex, +77%); DRAM contract prices holding after +90% q/q; Hormuz de-escalation with Brent <$75. Enter in thirds, require ≥2 independent green lights.

### [A] Samsung Electronics 1st preferred — `005935.KS`
- **Thesis:** Buy the world's dominant memory franchise at ~5x forward earnings on record supercycle numbers, through the preferred at a record-wide spread to common — two mean-reversions (panic + pref discount) stacked on one instrument, with a ₩90tn buyback program as the potential spread-closer.
- **Key numbers:** Pref ₩176,200 (Jul 13, -9.3% on the day; 52w 51,700–243,500) vs common ₩255,000 (-10.5%) = common 44.7% above pref; the gap hit a **record 54% on Jul 7** (DS Inv & Sec; was 26.5% at start of year — ETF flows into common only). Q2-26 prelim OP ~₩89.4tn (+1,810% y/y) on rev ~₩171tn; Q1 DS division OP ₩53.7tn (94% of total); DRAM/NAND blended prices +90%+ q/q; HBM4 shipping, reportedly sold out through 2027; common ~5x forward P/E vs 14x historical (Benzinga). Returns: ₩9.8tn annual dividend + 50% of FCF (2024–26 program) + planned ₩90tn 3-yr buyback (Jun 24 announcement).
- **Catalyst:** Buyback execution details (board approval, whether prefs are included — prefs are the rational purchase at a 45-54% discount); Q2 final results + shareholder-return announcement late July; foreign-flow reversal (foreign ownership at 16-yr low = coiled spring); pref spread mean-reversion toward the ~26% Jan level.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** This IS the epicenter of the Meta shock — if hyperscaler capex guides get cut, "5x peak earnings" becomes 15x mid-cycle fast (classic memory value trap; Q2 gross margins are unsustainable by construction); the ₩90tn buyback is earmarked to fund employee stock bonuses (154tn gross program), so cancellation/accretion is NOT assured — verify before crediting it; pref discount can persist for years (it widened all year); -10% days are still happening.
- **Next steps:** Wait for the stabilization checklist above (esp. capex guides + DRAM pricing); verify buyback mechanics from the actual disclosure (prefs eligible? cancellation?); model mid-cycle OP (₩35-45tn?) and re-check the multiple; track the daily disparity ratio — historical mean-reversion episodes (2016, 2020) closed 15-20pts in 6-12 months.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-13 · event live, do not knife-catch

<a id="12-financials-complexity-discount-float"></a>

## 12. Financials Complexity Discount & Float

**Why the edge exists:** Financials with opaque accounting screen terribly even when statutory cash generation is strong: LDTI hedge marks make annuity writers' GAAP book value and EPS nearly meaningless, Level 3 fair-value rollforwards (litigation finance) are unauditable at a glance, AOCI bond marks get treated as permanent capital destruction even though they pull to par on a knowable schedule, and holdco/subsidiary structures hide listed-stake value behind scary legacy liabilities. Generalists and quants avoid these names, passive flows underweight them, and the few sector specialists face career risk owning "black boxes" after 2008/2023 — so price discovery is slow. The discount closes not through earnings beats but through mechanical processes the market finds boring: buybacks executed below book/NAV, AOCI accretion, subsidiary dividends up-streamed, and corporate events (sales, reinsurance deals, conservatorship exits). Mid-2026 adds a cyclical layer: property and reinsurance pricing softened sharply at 1/1/26 renewals while casualty stays firm, meaning the market is starting to punish all insurers cyclically just as the balance-sheet-repair names are inflecting — widening the complexity discount independent of fundamentals.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Screen financials for P/TBV < 1.0 with ROTCE > 8%, and life insurers for price < 0.7x ADJUSTED book (ex-AOCI, ex-hedge marks) — reported GAAP book is useless for annuity writers; pull adjusted BVPS from quarterly statistical supplements (8-K exhibits), not data vendors.
- Free-capital-generation yield screen: statutory free capital generation (disclosed in earnings decks of JXN, LNC, CRBG, EQH) divided by market cap > 12%, combined with RBC ratio > 400% and active buyback below book.
- Sum-of-the-parts trigger: parent's stake in a LISTED subsidiary worth more than the parent's entire market cap (the Genworth/Enact model). Rebuild the list quarterly from 13D/G filings and holdco 10-K org charts.
- Bank pull-to-par: from 10-Q securities tables, compute AOCI accretion schedule (weighted-average maturity of AFS/HTM book vs current TBV discount); target banks below TBV where >60% of AOCI accretes back within 3 years and CRE/multifamily criticized-loan migration is flat or improving. S&P Global Market Intelligence bank screens and Mercer Capital's Bank Watch are good replenishment sources.
- P&C cycle position: track CIAB commercial rate survey, MarketScout barometer, and Gallagher Re / Guy Carpenter 1/1 renewal reports quarterly. As property softens (10-30% decreases on shared/layered property in 2026; double-digit risk-adjusted cat reinsurance declines at 1/1/26) and casualty stays firm, favor specialty/casualty-weighted books and check Schedule P reserve triangles for redundancy before trusting any combined ratio.
- Buyback-below-book cannibals: from 10-Q share counts, flag financials retiring >5% of shares annually at <0.9x book/NAV — the mechanical value transfer is the catalyst when no other exists.
- Event pipeline: GSE conservatorship exit headlines (FNMA/FMCC — stalled as of June 2026 per CNN, but any FHFA/Treasury action reprices instantly), demutualizations and MHC thrift conversions (S&P MHC list), insurers knocked below book by single cat events, and appellate dockets (PACER) for litigation-finance names where a court date is the catalyst.
- Follow the specialists: 13Fs/13Ds of financials-focused activists and funds (e.g., HoldCo Asset Management's public KeyCorp letter) for pre-vetted complexity-discount ideas.

<a id="13-secular-demand-priced-as-cyclical"></a>

## 13. Secular Demand Priced as Cyclical

**Why the edge exists:** These businesses were genuinely cyclical for decades, so quant screens, sell-side models, and investor muscle memory automatically apply trough/mid-cycle multiples the moment any headline wobble appears (ACA subsidy expiry, PVC price normalization, a shipyard margin miss, an 'AI capex pause' scare) — even when order books now stretch 5-30 years (gas turbines sold out through 2029-30, HII backlog 4x revenue, transformer lead times of 128-144 weeks). The re-rating has been extremely uneven: the first-derivative names everyone can see (GE Vernova, Powell, Argan at 63x, Hammond at 40x, NWPX up 3x, Centrus, Babcock at 25x) absorbed all the flows, while the second-derivative layer — hospitals, conduit makers, waterworks distributors, small regulated utilities, shipyards — still trades at or below historical median multiples because each carries a specific, headline-heavy but quantifiable near-term fear. Structural buyers agree the layer is mispriced: GIP/EQT is taking AES private at a 40%+ premium, and index/governance screens exclude family-controlled names like UHS, keeping natural buyers away. Policy-headline risk (Medicaid, ACA, DOGE) makes these positions career-uncomfortable for institutions on a quarterly clock, which is exactly why the duration of the demand stream goes uncapitalized.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Backlog-vs-multiple screen: companies where disclosed backlog or ASC 606 remaining performance obligations exceed 2x revenue (or book-to-bill >1.2 for 4+ straight quarters) while forward P/E or EV/EBITDA is below the company's own 5-10 year median; pull backlog tables from 10-K/10-Q Item 1 and RPO footnotes (GEV, HII, POWL, AGX all flagged here before re-rating)
- Utility load-growth watch: read quarterly earnings decks and IRP/rate-case filings of sub-$15B regulated utilities for 'large load electric service agreement' announcements and retail sales growth guidance >3%/yr; buy the ones still at <16x forward before the re-rate (Evergy re-rated within two quarters of its 2.5 GW ESA disclosure; hunt the next BKH/OGE/IDA-type name); FERC Form 714 and PJM/SPP interconnection queues are the leading indicators
- Gas-turbine food chain mapping: GE Vernova/Siemens/MHI sold out through 2029-30 means every incremental data-center MW flows to EPCs, switchgear, conduit, transformers, and HV cable; work down the bill of materials and screen each layer's multiple vs its 10-yr median (Atkore is the current trough; Prysmian/NKT/Encore-successors, Mueller Industries, EnerSys worth ongoing checks)
- Hospital policy-fear screen: track CMS ACA effectuated-enrollment data and state Medicaid supplemental/directed-payment approvals (each approval is an EPS event); screen providers trading >30% below 10-yr median P/E with buybacks retiring >5% of shares annually (UHS, THC today; CYH optionality); read the payer-mix table in every 10-Q
- Nuclear fuel cycle: monitor UxC/Cameco month-end spot ($85/lb June 2026) vs term price — term above spot signals the utility contracting cycle is alive; watch DOE HALEU task orders, NRC license extensions/uprates, and Kazatomprom sulphuric-acid-constrained guidance; the cheap entry now is producers whose realized prices lag contract repricing, not physical trusts (Yellow Cake flipped to NAV premium)
- Defense re-armament: read the US Navy 30-year shipbuilding plan, Columbia/Virginia-class milestone awards, and NATO 3.5%/5% GDP commitment national budget documents; screen for record order books (order book/revenue >1.5x) with P/E <15x — most of Europe has re-rated past this, so focus on US shipbuilding, energetics/ammunition inputs, and MRO layers
- Water: EPA Drinking Water Infrastructure Needs Survey (~$625B/20yr) and state revolving fund appropriations set the demand floor; screen pipe/valve/distribution names below 10-11x EV/EBITDA and watch muni bond issuance volumes as the spending leading indicator (MWA at ~$23 vs $27.67 consensus fair value is the current marginal name)
- Private-capital takeout signal: track infra PE (GIP/BlackRock, EQT, Blackstone, Brookfield) acquisitions of listed power/water assets (AES at $15/sh, $33.4B EV closing late 2026); screen remaining IPPs and utilities trading below replacement cost or regulated asset base for the next target
- Form 4 insider-buying clusters in de-rated capital goods, hospital, and utility names — cross-reference with the backlog screen above
