- **Kill criteria / risks:** Margins are structurally impaired by labor churn — every quarter of 5% margins extends the 'show-me' period; fixed-price cost overruns on early Columbia boats; continuing-resolution budget chaos delaying milestone payments; the stock has already re-rated from ~$250 (2024 lows) so a margin stumble means severe multiple compression
- **Next steps:** Read Q1 2026 10-Q contract-cohort disclosures and cumulative catch-up adjustments; model EPS at 7%, 8%, 9% shipbuilding margins on 2027-28 revenue; track Newport News hiring/attrition data points on calls; compare vs General Dynamics Marine (GD at ~$348, ~21x — similar multiple, more diversified) to pick the better sub exposure

- **Kill criteria / risks:** This is no longer statistically cheap — a spot-price fade to $65 kills the multiple and the catch-up story simultaneously; Kazakh government stake and potential export/tax changes; shipments transit Russia (St. Petersburg route) — sanctions escalation strands volumes; sulphuric acid shortage cuts production below guidance
- **Next steps:** Pull the H1 2026 results (August) realized-price vs spot table and model the contract roll-off curve; quantify the Trans-Caspian route capacity vs Russian route dependence; compare EV/lb of reserves vs Cameco and the term price; decide whether the geopolitical discount at ~20x TTM is adequate — if not, pass and revisit on a spot correction

<a id="14-capital-structure-arbitrage-post-reorg-equities"></a>

## 14. Capital-Structure Arbitrage & Post-Reorg Equities

**Why the edge exists:** Different investor bases are contractually or behaviorally forced into different layers of the same issuer: convert arbs sell when the equity delta dies, retail income buyers dump preferreds on headline dividend risk, and credit funds handed post-reorg equity are mandate-forced sellers of stock they never wanted. Post-bankruptcy equities compound this with fresh-start accounting that breaks every screen, zero sell-side coverage, and no index membership, so nobody's job is to price them for 6-18 months (WW, Wolfspeed, and Keenova all fit this in 2025-26). The mispricing persists because the fix requires reading dockets, indentures, and waterfalls — labor-intensive, size-constrained work that large funds cannot deploy against and passive money cannot see. The edge decays only when a listing, index add, refinancing, or exchange forces all investor bases to agree on one price, which is exactly the catalyst you underwrite.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Track Chapter 11 emergences monthly via Kroll/Epiq/Stretto claims-agent sites, bankruptcydata.com, and Octus (Reorg); flag every Form 8-A registration and fresh-start 10-K, then diary 6-12 months forward for the overhang-clearing window
- Screen for post-emergence S-1/424B3 resale registrations by selling stockholders (e.g., Wolfspeed's 24.1M-share resale shelf) — registered creditor shares quantify the overhang and its expiry
- Busted convert screen: converts priced <85% of par with conversion premium >75%, issuer cash + revolver > all maturities through the convert's own maturity, and converts as the only funded debt; pull prices via FINRA TRACE, verify terms in the original 8-K/indenture
- Preferred/baby-bond screen on QuantumOnline, preferredstockchannel.com, and Innovative Income Investor: cumulative issues at 60-85% of par, coupon covered >3x, plus fixed-to-float resets stepping up within 12 months (check reset spread vs SOFR and LIBOR-fallback language)
- Follow 13F/13D/13G of serial post-reorg and cap-structure holders: Two Seas Capital, HG Vora, Silver Point, Oaktree, Elliott, Castlelake — their new small-cap positions are often creditor-conversion stubs
- Credit-equity dislocation screen: issuers with any bond <70 cents while equity market cap exceeds 2x the total bond-market discount (or the inverse); confirm with same-day TRACE and equity closes, then do the waterfall
- Monitor exchange offers, consent solicitations, and sub-par tender 8-Ks daily (e.g., AMC Networks, Sarepta, MARA all executed discounted exchanges/buybacks in 2025-26) — issuers buying their own debt at a discount mark the cheap layer for you
- Watch UK Restructuring Plans and schemes (Practice Statement letters, The Gazette) plus US Chapter 15 recognitions — NFE-style cases create both pre-close bond entries and post-close orphan equities
- Diary Russell reconstitution (June) and S&P index adds for seasoned post-reorgs — index inclusion is the terminal catalyst that ends the orphan discount

**Candidates:**

### [A] WW International (WeightWatchers) — post-reorg equity — `WW (Nasdaq)`
- **Thesis:** 42-day prepack (emerged 6/24/25) wiped $1.15B of $1.6B debt and handed 91% of the equity to lenders who never wanted it; a ~$170M market cap orphan with a growing clinical (GLP-1 telehealth) segment hidden inside a shrinking legacy subscription business.
- **Key numbers:** Market cap ~$163-183M (late June 2026, verified); creditors received 91% of new equity, old holders 9% via 1-for-93 conversion (verified); Q1'26 clinical revenue +32% y/y, total revenue -10%, ARPU +13% (verified); company prepaying $40M of the exit term loan (verified); remaining exit debt ~$400-465M (unverified exact figure).
- **Catalyst:** Creditor-holder distribution selling exhausting itself; continued term-loan paydown; clinical segment crossing over legacy decline; possible index inclusion/sell-side initiation at this size only after re-rating.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Melting ice cube: legacy subscriber decline outruns clinical growth; Lilly/Novo direct-to-consumer channels disintermediate WW's telehealth; residual leverage on a small EBITDA base; brand impairment is permanent.
- **Next steps:** Pull the plan of reorganization and 8-K exit capital structure from Kroll docket; identify the 91% holders (13D/G filings — check HG Vora and other lenders) and whether lockups/registration statements signal selling; model clinical unit economics vs. legacy attrition from Q1'26 10-Q; compute EV/FCF assuming clinical stabilizes revenue by 2027.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

- **Thesis:** Junior preferreds at ~52% of par are the seniormost private claim in a politically-driven recap where the administration has publicly floated an IPO; the prefs win in almost any release scenario that respects the waterfall, while the commons carry the dilution risk — the 2026 lull after the IPO was pushed past the midterms has knocked the crowd out.

- **Kill criteria / risks:** Perpetual limbo — no dividends while you wait; a recap structure that lawyers around the juniors (new capital layered senior, receivership threats); political reversal after 2028. This is policy risk, not credit risk.
- **Next steps:** Rebuild the waterfall under 3 scenarios (par take-out, 60-80% exchange into common, status quo); track FHFA capital-rule amendments and Treasury statements; compare FNMAS vs lower-dollar-price series for convexity; size as an option, not a bond.

- **Kill criteria / risks:** Servicing revenue decline is secular, not cyclical — the post-reorg re-levering-into-broken-model trap; CCC+ means the 2027 wall could force restructuring round two where even reinstated notes take pain; SEK/EUR mismatch.
- **Next steps:** Pull post-recap capital structure from the reorganisation plan (note tranches, coupons, maturities, security); get indicative prices on the 2027-2030 notes; model coverage on Q1'26 run-rate EBIT after the equity raise; treat equity as a funded option only after the raise prices.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

- **Thesis:** Rural broadband ex-darling suspended its dividend, took a 2025 profitability hit, and just refinanced its 2026 converts with revolver borrowings — the 2028 converts at a discount are a two-year bet that a still-cash-generative broadband monopoly footprint covers one more maturity, priced by an investor base (growth-convert arbs) that has abandoned the name.

- **Kill criteria / risks:** This is the explicit deserved-discount candidate: fixed-wireless and fiber competition may permanently impair rural broadband economics; the revolver refinancing subordinates converts to a growing secured/bank layer; 2028 wall arrives before stabilization proves out.
- **Next steps:** TRACE the 1.128% '28s for price/YTM; read Q1'26 10-Q for revolver capacity, secured vs unsecured priority, and covenant headroom; model broadband sub/ARPU trajectory vs the $1.36B 3-5yr wall; compare vs owning the term loan if converts don't offer 300bps+ pickup.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

- **Key numbers:** Abra ~80% of common and preferred post-emergence (verified); ~$1.6B debt converted/extinguished plus ~$850M other obligations (verified); liquidity ~$900M at emergence (verified); exit financing $1.25B from Castlelake/Elliott (verified); current market cap and float unverified.

- **Kill criteria / risks:** Controlled-company governance — Abra can extract value via related-party debt and dilution rather than sharing it with minorities; BRL and jet fuel volatility; Brazilian airline industry's serial-restructuring history (this is GOL's chronic model, the re-levering trap).
- **Next steps:** Track the capital increase terms and subscription price on B3/CVM filings; read the plan for Abra's debt instruments sitting above the equity; only engage at a discount to the raise price with the float/liquidity constraint sized (position likely capped at low single-digit $M).
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

<a id="15-emerging-frontier-markets-deep-value"></a>

## 15. Emerging & Frontier Markets Deep Value

**Why the edge exists:** Whole markets get orphaned for reasons unrelated to individual business quality: index classification (frontier-vs-EM mandates cannot own Vietnam until FTSE's 21-Sep-2026 effective date, no matter how obvious the upgrade), currency trauma and capital-control memory (Argentina, Turkey) that keeps institutional owners out until AFTER the re-rating, career risk (no PM gets fired for skipping Kazakhstan at 3x earnings, everyone gets fired for owning it into a devaluation), and forced-seller dynamics (years of EM fund outflows sell the good companies alongside the bad). The catalysts are public and dated — rate-setting calendars, index review dates, IMF reviews, takeover-code rulings — but the constrained capital cannot front-run them, so unconstrained fundamental buyers get paid for doing country-level and governance-level homework. The persistent trap that funds the alpha: some markets are cheap for permanent reasons (expropriation, minorities never seeing the cash), so the edge is separating cheap-with-a-clock from cheap-forever, which requires reading filings most screeners never open.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Index reclassification pipeline: track FTSE Russell semi-annual country classification reviews (March/September) and MSCI June review + May 2027 Greece DM implementation; for each promoted market, pull the provisional constituent list and screen for names with foreign-ownership-limit room (FTSE's Vietnam FAQ lists the 28-32 eligible stocks and weights)
- Country-cycle screen: markets with trailing P/E < 9 (Ibovespa ~9x mid-2026) where the central bank has STARTED cutting (BCB began March 2026) — buy the domestic rate-sensitives (exchanges, rental/leasing, utilities), not the exporters; avoid markets where cuts are promised but not started (Turkey at 37% policy rate, disinflation stalled by the 2026 energy shock)
- HKEX governance-catalyst scan: read every scheme-of-arrangement/merger-by-absorption announcement (HKEX headline feed) and every SFC Takeovers Executive statement; the Dongfeng H-share privatization (completed March 2026, ~0.3x book, cash + VOYAH shares) and the HK$1.5bn Giordano settlement (Feb 2026) prove both catalyst types pay; screen H-shares and family companies at <0.5x book with net cash > 50% of market cap AND controller stake 24-75%
- Yield-with-buyback screen on London/US-listed EM GDRs/ADRs: dividend yield > 10% plus an active repurchase program (Halyk's $50m GDR buyback runs to Oct 2026) — the buyback is the tell that the controller cares about the listed price
- Closed-end country funds at >10% NAV discounts with dated tender programs (VEIL's three 10% tenders at 3% discount to NAV) — often the cleanest FX/repatriation wrapper for a foreign fund in pre-upgrade markets
- Argentina reversal-risk monitor: the sovereign curve prices the regime question directly — mid-2026 a bond maturing Oct-2028 (post-Milei) yields ~8.9% vs ~5.1% for 2027 paper; when that 380bp gap compresses, add to the Argentine cluster; IMF EFF review dates and monthly crawling-band changes are the calendar
- Sizing rule (treat country risk as one bet): cap each country cluster at 8-10% of NAV at cost and any single name at 4%; within Argentina assume intra-cluster correlation of ~1.0 in the downside scenario (peso/regime event hits banks, energy and utilities together); Vietnam event risk is binary but bounded — size the pair (broker + fund wrapper) as one 5-6% position; HK names carry idiosyncratic catalysts and can be sized independently; hold no more than 3 clusters at full size simultaneously
- Repatriation check before any position: confirm ADR/GDR line liquidity vs local line, dividend conversion mechanics (Argentina still runs a crawling FX band; Vietnam requires local custody + trading code), and withholding tax; if cash cannot verifiably exit, the position is a trade, not an investment

**Candidates:**

### [A] Localiza Rent a Car — `RENT3 (B3, São Paulo); thin US OTC only — use local line`
- **Thesis:** Brazil's dominant car-rental/fleet operator, a compounder whose equity has been crushed by the 14-15% Selic because its business is a spread on funding cost; multiple normalizes as the easing cycle (started March 2026) proceeds.

- **Kill criteria / risks:** August 2026 pause is live (BCB flagged worsening inflation ~5% in 2H26 from the energy shock); used-car price deflation compressing Seminovos disposal margins; leverage makes equity a levered bet on the spread between ROIC and Selic; election going anti-market
- **Next steps:** Read 1Q26 earnings release and model per-car economics (depreciation per car, rental yield) vs cost of debt; quantify EPS sensitivity per 100bp of Selic; verify current net debt/EBITDA and debt maturity ladder from the ITR filing; compare against Movida to confirm the quality premium is intact

<a id="16-closed-end-fund-trust-listed-pe-discounts"></a>

## 16. Closed-End Fund / Trust / Listed-PE Discounts

**Why the edge exists:** These wrappers are owned mostly by retail and legacy wealth-platform accounts with no natural arbitrageur: the assets are worth NAV but nobody can force conversion, so apathy, fee drag, and shrinking natural demand let prices sit 10-46% below NAV. The gap closes only when someone with a lever acts — activists (Saba, Karpus, Bulldog, Metage), boards forced into tenders/wind-downs/mergers by continuation votes, or contractual term dates — and each event has a definable date, making pull-to-NAV IRR computable in a way generalists rarely underwrite. The edge persists because campaigns are slow, position sizes are liquidity-capped, and institutions cannot own sub-scale vehicles; it is currently amplified by a structural shrinkage wave (record £18.9bn of net assets exited the UK trust sector in 2025, 27 trusts disappeared, £8.9bn of strategic reviews/wind-downs/mergers in the 2026 pipeline) and by the FCA's CCI disclosure regime (live April 6, 2026), which fixed the cost-disclosure distortion that had made wealth platforms forced sellers of trusts. Caveat: the US Supreme Court's June 11, 2026 ruling that ICA Section 47(b) has no private right of action removed one activist lever, so US campaigns now rest on proxy fights and tender pressure rather than litigation.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- US: screen CEFConnect/CEFData weekly for CEFs at >12% discount; cross-reference EDGAR Schedule 13D/13D-A filings by Saba Capital Master Fund, Karpus, Bulldog/Special Opportunities Fund, Almitas, Cox Capital; flag every PREC14A/DEFC14A (contested proxy) and SC TO-I (tender) in the CEF universe.
- US term funds: maintain a list of target-term/term CEFs with contractual termination inside 36 months (from N-2 prospectus term provisions); compute pull-to-NAV IRR = (NAV x (1 - wind-up cost) / price)^(1/years) - 1, adjusted for fee drag and credit NAV drift; watch BlackRock's quarterly discount-management-program measurement periods, which mechanically trigger tenders.
- US consolidation: track sponsor-led fund mergers (Nuveen state-muni roll-ups into NMZ completed April 27, 2026; NPV/NMS into NZF pending) — buy the target fund when its discount is wider than the acquirer's, since mergers close NAV-for-NAV.
- UK: monitor LSE RNS daily for the phrases 'strategic review', 'managed wind-down', 'tender offer', 'combination', and 'continuation vote'; QuotedData morning briefings, Citywire Investment Trust Insider, and the AIC news feed aggregate these; keep the 2026 meeting calendar (EOT wind-up meetings listed in the dossier for July 28 / August 7, subject to circular verification; Fidelity Emerging Markets late 2026; Schroder BSC Social Impact; Greencoat UK Wind; US Solar Fund).
- UK holder registers: check RNS Section 793/holdings disclosures for Saba (note its 3-year standstill on up to 9 trusts post-Herald), Metage, Boussard & Gavaudan, City of London IM (Karpus-style UK players), and wealth-platform consolidation flows (Rathbones/IW&I, RBC Brewin) that create forced selling in sub-£300m trusts.
- Listed PE NAV credibility test: for each fund-of-funds, compile realized exit uplift vs prior carrying value over trailing 24 months from annual/interim reports (HVPE, PIN, ICGT, NBPE all disclose this), commitment coverage ratio (undrawn commitments vs cash + credit line + 12m expected distributions), and secondary-market pricing for buyout LP stakes (Jefferies and Campbell Lutyens semi-annual secondary reports) as an independent check on marks.
- Traps checklist before any position: (1) is the NAV marked by a related-party adviser (HFRO/NexPoint pattern)? (2) does the discount-control mechanism have a hard trigger or board discretion? (3) does the board have adviser-aligned trustees and a classified structure/control-share defense? (4) what is realistic daily liquidity — cap position at ~10-15 trading days of volume.

<a id="17-royalty-streaming-toll-collection-models"></a>

## 17. Royalty, Streaming & Toll-Collection Models

**Why the edge exists:** Royalties are very-long-duration, inflation-linked, zero-capex claims on gross revenue, but GAAP depletion/amortization makes them screen as expensive (Royalty Pharma at 29x GAAP P/E vs ~9x cash receipts) or as ordinary E&P/miner/pharma comps, so quant screens and generalist PMs systematically misclassify them. The vehicles are mostly small, index-orphaned, K-1/trust-structured, or externally managed, so the natural arbitrage — buying the whole vehicle — requires control transactions; 2025-26 proved the gap with a wave of marks (Viper-Sitio $4.1B, WhiteHawk-PHX cash take-private, Royal Gold-Sandstorm $3.5B, Ligand-XOMA $739M, Concord-Round Hill, Irenic's bid for Reservoir), each struck well above where the public units traded. The discount persists because closing it takes either years of holding for distributions or a control event, because post-Hipgnosis skepticism taints all listed royalty NAVs, and because some managers keep issuing equity below NAV to grow fee bases, validating the market's distrust of the cohort even where individual NAVs are conservative.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- O&G minerals screen: US/Canada-listed mineral/royalty vehicles (KRP, DMLP, BSM, SBR, FRU, PSK, VNOM) with EV/annualized distributable cash flow < 8x and payout >=75%; mark each against private-deal $/net-royalty-acre and PDP PV-10 multiples from Enverus deal writeups and the Viper-Sitio / WhiteHawk-PHX proxies (SC TO-T, DEFM14A filings)
- Watch SEC form types that mark royalty assets: SC 13E-3 (take-privates), SC TO-T (tenders), DEFM14A fairness opinions — the banker DCFs inside are free third-party NAV marks for identical public assets
- Junior mining royalty cohort: maintain a monthly P/NAV table (spot vs consensus) for VOXR, ELE, MTA, GROY, ECOR, Sailfish, Empress; flag anything <0.8x P/NAV whose first royalty payments start within 12 months (cross-check operator 43-101 technical reports and quarterly production releases); after Royal Gold-Sandstorm and EMX-Elemental, assume the cohort shrinks — own the probable targets, not the acquirers
- Pharma royalties: rebuild royalty-by-royalty DCFs from 10-K/AIF royalty tables using consensus drug forecasts (Evaluate/Visible Alpha); screen for small caps whose single contracted royalty DCF > market cap; monitor SEDAR+ NCIB notices (DRI model) and any externally managed vehicle announcing internalization — internalization events preceded re-ratings at RPRX and DRI
- Music/media: track private catalog multiples (15-25x NPS) via MBW/Billboard/Chartlex deal trackers and the appraisal decks (Citrin Cooperman/Shot Tower) filed in proxies; screen listed owners trading <12x NPS; follow activist 13Ds (Irenic-Reservoir template) and trust 10-Ks with royalty-audit or underpayment disputes (Mills-EMI) as event catalysts
- Toll misc: franchise royalty funds (BPF.UN, PZA.TO, KEG.UN) — only act when payout <95% and units yield >8%, otherwise fairly priced; land/water/surface royalties — use TPL and LandBridge deal metrics as marks for cheaper land-royalty stubs; screen conglomerates where a royalty segment's standalone DCF covers >80% of EV (Altius Minerals, Innoviva)
- Trap checklist before any position: (1) reserve/royalty life <10 years but priced on current yield as a perpetuity; (2) royalty junior to a distressed single-asset operator (check operator credit, not just the royalty terms); (3) external manager issuing equity below NAV to grow fee base — check 3-year share count vs NAV/share trend; (4) GAAP-vs-cash bridge: always rebuild cash receipts from the royalty table, never trust EPS

**Candidates:**

### [A] Reservoir Media — `RSVR (NASDAQ)`
- **Thesis:** Only remaining US-listed pure music IP owner trades ~26% below its own third-party catalog appraisal (~$14/share) with a live unsolicited bid from Irenic Capital ($10-11/share, Feb 2026) and a special committee running a strategic-alternatives process since May 2026.
- **Key numbers:** Price $10.32, mkt cap $677M (7/1/26, verified); FY2026 revenue $175.7M +11%, FY27 guide $186-191M (verified); third-party catalog appraisal ~$1.3B (~$14/share) as of 3/31/25 (verified); Irenic bid $10-11/share ~= $1.1-1.2B incl. debt (verified); net debt ~$350-400M (unverified)
- **Catalyst:** Special committee outcome (sale, take-private, or levered recap) — decision plausible H2 2026; private catalog comps 15-19x NPS vs RSVR implied ~12x (appraisal gap closes on any transaction)
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Founding Khosrowshahi family control can block any sale indefinitely (control stake unverified); appraisal discount rate may be too low post-Hipgnosis; streaming royalty growth deceleration; leverage against floating rates
- **Next steps:** Read the 14D9/8-K trail on the Irenic proposal and special committee mandate; pull the FY2026 10-K catalog appraisal assumptions (discount rate, decay curves) and rebuild NAV at 10-12% discount rates; check proxy for family voting control and change-of-control provisions in the credit facility; compare implied NPS multiple to 2025-26 private catalog deals (Chartlex tracker)
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

<a id="18-earnings-power-hidden-by-accounting-gaap-distortions"></a>

## 18. Earnings Power Hidden by Accounting (GAAP Distortions)

**Why the edge exists:** GAAP treats value-creating investment — customer acquisition, segment incubation, pre-opening costs, purchase-accounting amortization, accelerated depreciation — identically to operating expense, so reported EPS is structurally wrong as a measure of economic profit for these firms. Quant value screens, factor models, and index value/growth taxonomies all consume the reported numbers, so these stocks are systematically mis-sorted: they screen 'expensive' or 'unprofitable' on P/E while being cheap on owner earnings, and passive/factor flows never correct it. Extracting the truth requires hand-rebuilding the P&L from segment footnotes, cohort disclosures, and purchase price allocations — labor-intensive work that doesn't scale, which is why the wedge persists until the spend rolls off, disclosure changes, or an activist forces the harvest. The edge decays only at catalyst points (ramp completion, amortization schedule expiry, guidance to moderate growth spend), which are knowable in advance from filings.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- Screen: FCF yield >8% while GAAP P/E >18 or negative, AND amortization of acquired intangibles >30% of EBIT (pull from 10-K cash flow statement) — flags the amortization wedge; then check organic growth ex-acquisitions to kill 'maintenance M&A in disguise' (the OpenText test)
- Screen: GAAP P/E >40 with stable/positive EBITDA and D&A growing faster than revenue — flags over-depreciation / ramp-phase asset bases (the U-Haul pattern); compare depreciation rate on gross PP&E vs peer economic-life assumptions and listen for depreciation-rolloff guidance on calls
- Parse 10-K/20-F segment footnotes for the two-segment pattern: one segment with strong adj EBITDA + an 'investment' segment with losses >25% of the profitable segment's EBITDA (the Coupang pattern); rebuild consolidated P&L with the loss segment at zero and at disclosed long-term margin targets
- Maintain a list of 2024-2026 post-LBO relistings (Carlyle, EQT, Platinum, Hellman & Friedman exits) and read the S-1 purchase price allocation: large customer-relationship intangibles amortizing over 8-15 years create multi-year GAAP EPS suppression that quant taxonomies misclassify
- Restaurant/retail/industrial 10-Ks: sum pre-opening expense + disclosed new-unit deleverage, compute mature-unit margin vs consolidated margin; investigate when the gap exceeds 400bps and unit growth is decelerating (spend rolls off into reported numbers)
- Subscription/insurance names that disclose operating income before new-customer acquisition spend with multi-year retention cohorts (Trupanion pattern); validate cohort math against third-party data (state insurance filings, app-download data) — never accept management-only cohort math
- Catalyst watchlist: ASC 280-driven segment reporting changes, management guidance to 'moderate investment' or 'harvest', 13D filings on names with a GAAP/owner-earnings wedge, and scheduled amortization expiries from the intangibles footnote
- Kill-tests to run on every candidate before promotion: (1) rebuild with SBC as a real cost, (2) test whether R&D/CAC is actually maintenance by checking churn/organic decline, (3) reject adjusted-EBITDA addbacks that recur every year (restructuring, 'transformation' costs)

**Candidates:**

### [A] U-Haul Holding Company — `UHAL / UHAL.B (NYSE)`
- **Thesis:** GAAP net income collapsed to $83M (P/E ~157x) almost entirely from a transitory fleet-depreciation surge and disposal losses on COVID-era trucks, plus lease-up drag from a massive self-storage development pipeline carried at depreciated cost — owner earnings and asset value are multiples of what the income statement shows.
- **Key numbers:** Price $66.46, mkt cap $13.0B (7/1/26); FY2026 (ended 3/31/26) net income $83.1M vs $367.1M prior year; fleet depreciation +$186.6M YoY plus $104.5M disposal losses (vs $15M gain prior year); self-storage revenue +8.3% FY26; Shoen family control ~50%+ (unverified exact %); implied EV/EBITDA and storage NOI per sq ft unverified — must compute from 10-K
- **Catalyst:** Fleet depreciation cycle rolls off as over-priced 2021-23 truck purchases finish depreciating and used-truck resale market normalizes; storage portfolio lease-up converts development spend into visible NOI; any REIT-style disclosure or sum-of-parts event on the storage estate
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Fleet losses prove structural (EV transition, permanently weak used-truck values) rather than cyclical; storage overbuilding in Sunbelt keeps lease-up yields below cost of capital; family control means no catalyst forcing value recognition — can stay 'cheap' indefinitely; DIY-moving demand secularly tied to home turnover, which stays frozen
- **Next steps:** Read FY2026 10-K: split depreciation between fleet and real estate; build the bridge GAAP EPS -> owner EPS by (1) normalizing fleet depreciation to pre-2024 per-truck rates, (2) capitalizing storage lease-up deficit, (3) marking storage sq ft at private-market cap rates (compare Public Storage/Extra Space per-sq-ft values); model FY2028 EPS when disposal losses lap; verify Shoen ownership from proxy
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [A] SS&C Technologies — `SSNC (NASDAQ)`
- **Thesis:** Classic serial-acquirer amortization wedge: GAAP EPS $3.15 (2025) vs adjusted EPS $6.14, with the gap dominated by amortization of acquired customer-relationship intangibles that require no economic replacement spend given 95%+ revenue retention — stock trades ~9x true earnings while screening ~20x GAAP.
- **Key numbers:** Price $63.91, mkt cap $15.4B, down 21% (7/1/26); GAAP P/E 19.9 vs forward P/E 9.1; FY2025 GAAP EPS $3.15 vs adjusted $6.14; FY2026 adjusted EPS guidance raised to ~$6.90 midpoint (Q1-26: GAAP $0.91 vs adj $1.69); founder Bill Stone stake ~13% (unverified); net debt and FCF conversion unverified — pull from 10-K
- **Catalyst:** Amortization from older deals (Advent, DST, Intralinks) rolls off schedule; continued debt paydown shifts EV to equity; buybacks at 9x earnings are highly accretive; company raised 2026 guidance in April 2026
- **Kill criteria / risks:** The kill-test: if fund-administration revenue retention deteriorates, amortization IS economic and the adjusted number is fake — verify organic growth is positive ex-acquisitions; SBC addback must be reversed (real cost); leverage amplifies any core erosion; Stone succession risk
- **Next steps:** Build line-by-line bridge from 2025 10-K: GAAP net income + intangible amortization (tax-effected) - SBC = owner earnings; check intangibles footnote for amortization schedule by year; compute 5-year organic revenue growth ex-deals; verify Stone's stake and buyback pace from proxy and Q1-26 10-Q; compare EV/FCF vs Broadridge, FIS
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [A] Coupang — `CPNG (NYSE)`
- **Thesis:** Segment accounting hides core earnings power: Product Commerce (Korea e-commerce) generates positive adjusted EBITDA while the expensed Developing Offerings buildout (Eats, Taiwan, Farfetch) ran a -$329M loss in Q1-26 alone, dragging consolidated GAAP to a net loss — and a Nov-2025 data breach added temporary voucher/idle-capacity costs, giving a double discount on a stock now down 36%.
- **Key numbers:** Price $18.13, mkt cap $32.5B, down ~36% (7/1/26); Q1-26: revenue $8.5B (+8%), Product Commerce adj EBITDA $358M (5.0% margin, compressed ~100bps by breach fallout) vs Developing Offerings adj EBITDA -$329M (loss doubled YoY); consolidated net loss $266M; TTM revenue $35.1B, TTM net loss $165M
- **Catalyst:** Breach costs (vouchers, idle capacity) lap by early 2027 with management guiding margin expansion to resume 'next year'; Developing Offerings losses are discretionary — any moderation or Taiwan/Eats breakeven milestone flips consolidated optics; segment disclosure already lets analysts do the math once sentiment turns
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Korean regulatory fines and customer-trust damage from the data breach prove structural, not temporary (first single-digit revenue growth since IPO is the warning); Developing Offerings spend is escalating not maturing — losses doubled YoY, so the 'growth investment' claim needs proof of cohort economics in Taiwan/Eats; Product Commerce margin compression could be competitive (AliExpress/Temu in Korea), not incident-driven
- **Next steps:** Read Q1-26 earnings script and 10-Q: quantify one-time breach costs vs run-rate; rebuild P&L with Developing Offerings at zero and at management's long-term PC margin target (10%+ adj EBITDA was the prior framework — verify); track Korean PIPC penalty proceedings; model steady-state: PC EBITDA less normalized capex, taxed, vs $32.5B cap; compare vs pre-breach PC segment margins (Q1-25)
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-02

### [B] OpenText — `OTEX (NASDAQ/TSX)`
- **Thesis:** Amortization-of-acquired-intangibles wedge at an extreme: forward P/E 5.3 and ~$900M annualized FCF against a $5.5B market cap because GAAP EPS is crushed by Micro Focus purchase-accounting amortization — but this is also the domain's best test case for the trap, since the amortization is only non-economic if the maintenance-renewal base isn't melting.
- **Key numbers:** Price $22.85, mkt cap $5.54B, down 24% (7/1/26); GAAP P/E 11.2, forward P/E 5.25; FY26 YTD (9 mo) FCF $686M vs $563M prior year; Q3-FY26 non-GAAP EPS $3.19 YTD (+11.9%); net debt ~$5.5-6B (unverified — confirm from Q3 10-Q); organic revenue trend slightly negative (unverified exact %)
- **Catalyst:** Debt paydown mechanically transfers ~$500M+/yr of EV to equity; Micro Focus intangible amortization schedule steps down; buyback capacity at a double-digit FCF yield; any stabilization of cloud bookings re-rates the terminal-value assumption

<a id="19-demutualizations-mhc-structures-thrift-conversions"></a>

## 19. Demutualizations, MHC Structures & Thrift Conversions

*Added 2026-07-13 (tranche-2 sweep).*

**Why the edge exists:** The alpha is structural and regulatory, not informational. (1) Conversion math: in a mutual-to-stock conversion the IPO proceeds are ADDED to pre-existing equity that has no prior owners, and the independent appraisal (RP Financial et al.) deliberately prices the offering at roughly 50-70% of PRO FORMA tangible book — so a $10 subscription buys $10 of your own cash back plus a slice of a century of retained earnings for free. Regulators cap the pricing; depositors and ESOPs get priority; there is no economic seller on the other side. (2) Post-IPO, converted thrifts carry 20-30% TCE/assets, which crushes ROE and makes them screen terribly, keeping quant and institutional money out; most are $50-250M market caps with zero sell-side coverage and index-ineligible floats. (3) The 3-year federal anti-acquisition moratorium (12 CFR 192 / OCC-Fed policy) creates a datable catalyst: after year 1 they buy back stock (often 5-10% of shares per year below TBV, mechanically compounding TBV/share), and after year 3 they become acquirable — S&P Global data shows takeouts historically cluster at 1.2-1.6x TBV plus core-deposit premiums; sales below TBV are rare (Delanco at 98.7% was the exception). Live 2025-26 proof: William Penn (2021 class) sold to Mid Penn April 2025 (~$103-120M all-stock), CFSB Bancorp taken out by Hometown Financial at ~1.19x book Nov 2025, TC Bancshares to Colony. (4) MHC stubs: headline P/B is meaningless when the MHC owns 55-81% but waives its dividends (TFSL's MHC waived up to $1.27/share again on 7/7/2026 members' vote) — minority holders get the entire dividend stream and a mathematically favorable exchange ratio in any second step. (5) The 2023 rate shock left the 2021-2024 conversion classes trading at 0.7-0.9x TBV just as their moratoriums expire into the most permissive bank-M&A regulatory environment in a decade. The anomaly persists because the mechanics are odd, the names are tiny and boring, and the payoff requires 1-3 years of patience — exactly what institutional capital cannot arbitrage. Pipeline remains alive: Columbia Financial's $1.1B second step (subscription closed 6/16/26), Rhinebeck's second step (Q3 2026), CSB Financial/Community Savings Bank Chicago S-1 (member vote June 2026), and on the insurance side Mutual of Omaha's 2026 MIHC reorganization plus Old Republic's sponsored demutualization of Everett Cash Mutual (S-4 filed FY2026) — the latter two are not directly investable but signal the demutualization window reopening.

**How to hunt (replenishment screens):**
- P/TBV < 0.85 combined with TCE/assets > 15%: excess capital is both downside protection and buyback fuel; ignore P/E and ROE screens entirely at this stage.
- 3-year moratorium expiring within ~12 months: track exact conversion closing dates (2022-2023 IPO classes are ripe now — SRBK 9/19/2026, CPBI 10/2026, NBBK 12/2026; 2024 class ripens mid-late 2027).
- Buyback intensity after year 1: share count shrinking > 5%/yr below TBV (SRBK -9.4%, FDSB -8.9%) signals a board managing toward a sale rather than empire-building; stagnant share count + branch expansion is the anti-signal.
- Deposit franchise in an actively consolidating market (NJ/NY metro, New England, Chicago, New Orleans/Gulf South) where in-market acquirers pay core-deposit premiums; low-cost granular deposits matter more than loan growth.
- Second-step offerings: subscribe/participate at $10.00 whenever priced below ~80% of pro forma TBV (pull the appraisal from the prospectus); minority shares of the MHC subsidiary pre-announcement also work via the exchange ratio.
- MHC stubs ONLY where the MHC waives its dividend (TFSL; verify annual Fed non-objection): you collect the full payout on a minority float plus free second-step optionality; avoid stubs where the MHC takes the dividend.
- Pipeline sourcing: monitor SEC S-1s for 'plan of conversion', FDIC/OCC/Fed application lists, and open small deposit accounts at large healthy mutuals to bank subscription rights years in advance (the Klarman move).
- Alignment check: management/director buying in the IPO, ESOP + (post-year-1) MRP equity grants that vest faster on change of control; avoid conversions done to fund credit holes.

**Candidates:**

### [A] SR Bancorp (Somerset Regal Bank, Bound Brook NJ) — `SRBK (Nasdaq)`
- **Thesis:** Cleanest moratorium-expiry play in the market. Converted 9/19/2023 at $10.00 raising $90.6M while simultaneously acquiring Regal Bancorp ($431M assets), so it IPO'd as a real $1B-class NJ commercial bank, not a sleepy S&L. Trades at 0.76x book while retiring ~9.4% of its shares per year — every buyback below book compounds TBV/share. The 3-year federal anti-acquisition moratorium expires 9/19/2026, two months from now, in a NJ market that is visibly consolidating (Columbia/Northfield closing this month). A takeout at the historical 1.2-1.4x TBV range implies $30+ vs $18.93 today; if no deal, buybacks at 0.76x keep building value.
- **Key numbers:** Price $18.93 (7/10/26); BV/share $24.99; P/B 0.76x; mkt cap $146M; shares 7.73M, -9.4% y/y; IPO $10.00 on 9/19/2023 (9,055,172 shares, $90.6M); moratorium ends 9/19/2026. Sources: stockanalysis.com statistics 7/10/26; SR Bancorp 8-K 9/2023.
- **Catalyst:** 3-year anti-acquisition window opens 9/19/2026; continued tender/open-market buybacks below book; potential 13D activist filings immediately post-anniversary; NJ in-market M&A wave (Columbia-Northfield precedent).
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Board/management may choose independence (moratorium expiry is permission, not obligation); NJ CRE/multifamily credit; thin float and wide spreads (~$146M cap); earnings still modest so if no sale the stock can stay cheap; ESOP/foundation shares complicate activist pressure.
- **Next steps:** Read FY6/30/26 10-K and proxy for change-of-control payments, MRP vesting terms, and board age/tenure (retirement-age boards sell); verify current TCE/assets and remaining buyback authorization; set 9/19/2026 calendar trigger and watch EDGAR for 13D/G filings; size position within float constraints.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-13

- **Thesis:** The largest second-step conversion in years is pricing RIGHT NOW: up to 192.6M shares at $10.00 (min 142.4M), with the subscription offering ($925M-$1.1B in orders) closed 6/16/26, stockholder/depositor approvals received 6/25-6/29/26, and the residual firm-commitment underwritten offering launched the week of 7/6/2026. Second steps are structurally priced below pro forma TBV (appraisal convention ~70-85%), so buying at $10.00 in the syndicate gets the classic 'free book value' math on a $10B+ asset NJ franchise — and the concurrent all-stock absorption of Northfield ($5B+ assets, Staten Island/NJ deposits) adds in-market cost synergies from day one. Minority CLBK shares convert via an exchange ratio preserving ownership, giving a second entry route.

### [C] Hoyne Bancorp (Hoyne Savings, Chicago IL) — `HYNE (Nasdaq)`
- **Thesis:** Freshest conversion on the board: closed 12/3/2025 at $10.00 (7.935M shares, ~$79M), popped to $13.35 day one and now sits at $16.59 — yet still only 0.83x book because the offering added so much capital. Zero-leverage way to own the front end of the seasoning curve: buybacks become permissible December 2026, the equity-plan vote follows, and the moratorium expires December 2028. Chicago-area community bank consolidation (and nearby CSB Financial/Community Savings Bank converting in 2026) gives the metro two fresh conversion plays.
- **Key numbers:** Price $16.59 (7/10/26); BV/share $19.89; P/B 0.83x; mkt cap $134M; shares 8.10M; IPO closed 12/3/2025, 7,935,000 shares at $10.00, Nasdaq debut 12/4/2025 at $13.35; buybacks allowed from ~12/2026; moratorium ends ~12/2028. Sources: PRNewswire 12/2025; stockanalysis.com 7/10/26.
- **Catalyst:** First buyback authorization ~Dec 2026; equity incentive plan adoption; TBV/share compounding from redeployment of IPO cash; long-dated M&A option 12/2028.
- **Kill criteria / risks:** Longest wait in this list (2.5 years to the window); post-IPO pop already captured part of the discount; sub-scale Chicago thrift with legacy cost structure; earnings likely minimal near-term (ROE ~2-4%), so the stock is dead money if buybacks disappoint.
- **Next steps:** Verify pro forma TCE/assets from the prospectus; watch for the year-one buyback announcement (Dec 2026) as the add trigger; monitor the sister pipeline name CSB Financial Inc. (Community Savings Bank, Chicago — S-1 filed FY2026, member vote June 2026) and get the prospectus to order in its community offering.
- **Status:** backlog · added 2026-07-13
